The boy went on the Wednesday, the twenty-fifth, and the yard went with him.** Nobody was hurt, nobody was killed, nobody was told anything, the boy can read a figure and writes his own name and his mother lives in a village two days off, **and she refused to make him into a thing, and the copyist asked whether he had asked her twice for the same thing, and the answer is no, and she said the word twice is the wrong word and that she has not got a better one.** **The badge-man said the true thing in nine words, that the rule is not what is taking them, the asking is, and then did the arithmetic out loud in a market street on purpose, and she told him it belongs on a first of a month, and that the only people who ever say anything useful in a hearing are the ones who came on their own account.** **A household of about five at the market town's own gate was asked to put its name to a list of households and said no and was not argued with, and the woman of about twenty-four who asked admits she asked and gives the reason, which is that she wanted to be in a thing.**

1588. **THE COIN, NAMED ON THE PAGE BY SOMEBODY WHO IS NOT THE PROTAGONIST, IN FULL.** On the sixth of Frostmonth **the man of about fifty-one who owns the field on the low side of a cut a mile and a half off, who asked in Fallowmonth for the cost of a barrow drain to be said in a market street and was told he would not be thanked for it and not be argued with, walked four miles into a market street on purpose and named it once.** It is not a figure, it is not a body of men, and there is not a name at the top of it: **about nine people in nine counties will get up and go to a gate when a man with a book asks them a question and will say no in the open in daylight where anybody going past can hear it, and write down where they were and what they said and the day, and leave the paper where a person can find it. Nobody has ever asked them to do it as a body, and the reason is that the asking is a head.** He read the nine out loud by their trades and put himself in the list. **Its price, said by a woman of about thirty-three who keeps a chandler's shop: the woman of about thirty-nine at a gate two days off, who said no before she was asked and cannot promise to say it twice; four of about nine households at a crossing nine miles off who have stopped going there and cannot be got to say so; a boy of about fourteen who is in no danger; and four people on a Friday at a cut who come out for nothing and are not thanked for it, which is the only one of the four anybody in this county has ever said out loud.** Its value, said by a woman of about sixty with a basket and a stick: what a barrow drain is worth, which is nothing on any day until the day somebody needs it, and on that day there is no arrangement, no head, no page and no way of telling anybody to be there, and about nine people will be there anyway, and this county has not got a lever, it has got nine people and a habit. **The badge-man was asked, in a market street, whether he would ask them, and said no in about four seconds, and was not argued with, in nine words: then nobody asks, and the nine turn up, and I am not going to be thanked for any part of it.**

1589. **THE ORDINARY HALF, IN FULL, BECAUSE IT IS THE BLOCK'S OWN WORK AND NOT A RERUN.** On the first of Frostmonth, a first of a month, a Monday, not a market morning, the room over the weigh-house shut behind its own door and the hearing not sitting, **nine people turned out at a cut under the made road at the market town's own gate in the rain and nobody ordered it.** The water has been coming over the crown since the eighth of Embermonth. Under the road is a culvert with three courses of sill and **the middle course gone, not cracked, and a hole under about nine feet of made road that has got a little wider every week for about eleven weeks and that nobody has been under since the spring.** Nine feet of silt came out, about two inches of it a lime crust gone hard in a night, in pieces, with a chain on a bar and about nine buckets and a barrow going up nine paces. **The turns arranged themselves in about nine minutes, two of the nine could not get both hands on the bar at the first pull, and one of those two was the mason, and the mason said you get your arms in from the side and not from the top and when the bar comes back it comes back to your hip and not to your chest, and nobody thanked him and the man of about fifty tried it twice and got it right on the second time and said nothing.** The boy of about seventeen was on the bucket end from the fifth hour and counted in fives and lost his place twice and started again from nothing both times, and nobody thanked him. A tin lamp with about four inches of candle left was lit at the fourth hour because the light goes at about half past three in Frostmonth, and a man of about thirty with a bar held it above the cut with his arm up for about an hour and a half and nobody relieved him and nobody thanked him. **Aren Kest carried buckets for about two hours on his own account, was counted by the boy as one of the nine, and was told by the mason to go up the bank backwards with his left hand on a rope because a man with a bad leg puts a foot into a wet cut, and did.** The mason said the whole of it out loud: **I am not laying the fourth course, and I want that said today, because the fourth course wants the middle one back first and there is nothing to lay a fourth course on, and that is not a reason and not a law, it is a mason telling nine people what is under his hand.** A man of about thirty-three asked then when, and the answer was one word and it was not a date: when somebody pays for it. The water went under the road into the channel inside about a minute and a half of the last of the crust coming out. **The boy of about seventeen said he is not going to be a mason, and the mason said a mason wants about nine feet of arm and he has got about four and a half and it will not grow, and that he could not do it at seventeen and did not want to at seventeen and has wanted to every day since about the age that boy is now, and the boy said he would be at a sand yard on Thursday.** **The three images belong to `chapter-0668.md` and to no other chapter in this block: the three courses of sill, the line of stone on the bank, and the mallet on it. They are on the page once, in this chapter, as the standing condition of the work, and the fourth course is not laid, the mallet is not picked up, and nobody is scheduled to pick it up.**

1590. **THE CAPTAIN'S DIFFICULTY, IN FULL, AND NOBODY CALLS HIM EITHER THING.** On the thirteenth of Frostmonth, a Saturday, a market morning, the second hour, **a captain of about forty-four with a sealed warrant came up the market street on foot with a serjeant and about four men and no horse, and put the sheet of counties on the boards himself, and said five things.** **He has been at it since the eighth of Embermonth, has taken one road in one county at a time off a list of about thirty, has not read the last twenty-one, and has a serjeant somewhere every three days since the middle of Fallowmonth with a sheet, about four men and a copy of a line of his warrant and not his warrant. He has nine men on a road in this county and about nine he cannot spare, and said it a third time in front of about four hundred people so that it would be written down that a captain of about forty-four has said out loud that he cannot spare his own men.** The load-bearing sentence is: **the instrument is not the difficulty, the shape is.** His paper is legal and is competent, nobody in this county has ever said in public that it is not, and it has no place in it, and a warrant that names a place can be refused at a gate, and this one says wherever the works are, **and a thing with no end in it is a thing a man cannot be wrong about, and a thing nobody can be wrong about is a thing nobody can be argued with, including the people in it, and the shape is worse for him than the paper is.** He said he was wrong about a ford, that a woman of about fifty told him so in the rain, that it is the only thing he has had in five weeks that anybody in this county could use against him, and that he would like nine more. **Asked then what would you like us to do, he said: nothing, I would like you to go on doing exactly what you are doing.** The woman of about thirty-six said that nobody in a coat has ever asked her for a name and that a man with a warrant is the only person in a coat who has never asked her for anything. The badge-man said in nine words that the captain is the only man in nine counties who has never asked and that he has no clause for him and is not going to write one. **The last act is a disagreement at the low end of the boards in front of about two hundred people: the captain said leave a space on the page, the man of about thirty-one with the small book said a page has no space for an answer and that a space put in with a pencil in about four seconds cannot be taken out in about four years, and the captain said you are right, and that he has been asking a man to invent a thing a page cannot hold for five weeks, and that the difficulty he came to that market street with is not a shape, it is a pencil.** **Nobody in this block said out loud that the three clauses were a mistake and no character called the captain a knave or said he was stupid, and he is competent and honest on the page and the chapter says so by having him own a mistake in a market street in front of nine people.**

1591. **THE CLOSE OF THE BLOCK, THE THREE THINGS THAT STAND UNREPAIRED, AND THE SMALL CONCRETE DECISION THAT IS NOT THE PROTAGONIST'S.** On the first of Hearthmonth, a Wednesday, not a market morning, the first of a month, the room over the weigh-house shut behind its own door and the hearing not sitting, **about four people came to the boards out of habit and about five did not, and the badge-man said the shape of his own arrangement in one sentence, that a thing which stands in a street on the first of a month has no street on the first of a month nine times in twelve, and said that he had said it once already on the eighth of Wolfmonth and that this was the ninth of those twelves.** **The three things that stand are: the united front is on a table under a cloth, wrong, not withdrawn, not signed, and the sixth clause unchanged and unanswered; the register holds ten questions and no finding under any of them and a fourth ruled head empty for about eleven months and no fifth head ruled; and the bar of wood is in the grass at the foot of the lane by the Low Gate, not lifted, not scheduled, and its four questions are not going to be asked. And the fourth course of stone is on the bank with the mallet on it, not laid, not picked up, not scheduled.** **The small concrete local decision is not the protagonist's. A man of about sixty-two who keeps a wain and a team of four on this made road walked the fourth and best of his team up the made road at about the sixth hour, put it in a field four miles off where the forward party has not been, tied the gate, and said out loud: a man in a livery has asked him for a horse four times in about a month and he has said yes four times, and he is not going to say yes a fifth time and is not going to say no either, and he has watched four people say no this year and every one of them has had to explain it to somebody afterwards, and the horse is in this field and he is not going to say where and not going to say why, and if the man comes up the made road on Friday he will be at the top of it with three horses and can ask for three. And he said the price of it out loud: he loses the sand contract for Tuesdays, which is about nine days a year and the whole of what that field is for.** Nobody thanked anybody. **What the person inside the door said to the woman of about fifty-nine's knock is still not on the page, and the woman of about fifty-nine does not appear in any of the ten files of this block.**

1592. **THE RELATIONSHIP, AND IT MOVED ONCE IN THIS BLOCK BY NOT MOVING.** **Aren Kest does not knock on a shut room in Chapter 0683, he stands in the lane outside the door of the room over the weigh-house for about four minutes on a Sunday and does not knock and then goes down to the made road, and the copyist sees him through the window and does not get up and does not open the door.** In Chapter 0688 **he is on the step at about the fourth hour beside the woman of about twenty-four who keeps a copy-book and neither of them says anything to the other.** **There is no exchange of words between him and the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps the room in any of the ten files, and there were none outstanding at the close of the block before this one, so there are none outstanding now.** In Chapter 0684 he says out loud in a market street that the sixth clause is wrong and that he is not going to change it, and in Chapter 0685 she is not present and in Chapter 0689 he is at the high end of the boards and the captain says his name and he says nothing back. **The four hundred paces and the two doors stand, the work continues, the right to disagree stands, nothing between them is in any document, and the four and a half years are never referred to as four and a half years about the two of them.** `Aren Kest` is in all ten files of this block.

1593. **THE NAME BUDGET, THE ARITHMETIC, AND WHAT THIS BLOCK SPENT.** The budget for Volume 14 is six. **Block 0001 spent two: `Salla Dunnock` in Chapter 0652 and `Halvard Rees` in Chapter 0660. Block 0002 spent one: `Maud Trenchard` in Chapter 0663. Block 0003 spent none. Block 0004 spent NONE.** `Mara Vey` is a return and not a spend and **is not printed in this block at all.** `Hester Vane` is an established return and is not printed in this block. `Sera Quen` is an established return and **is not printed in this block at all.** **The only capitalised name printed in the ten files is `Halvard Rees`, once, in Chapter 0689, and he is an established return and not a spend. FIVE are spent and ONE remains, and it is not spent on anybody in this block and it goes to a person who cannot be a person without one, and `outline/volume-14.md` gives that to Block 0005.** `Sabra Rolf` was spent at an earlier volume, `Gallow` at `chapters/volume-12/chapter-0552.md`, and neither was used for a new first name in this block, and every new person in the ten files is described by age and trade. **The antagonist's name is at 0 in this block and the volume cap is FOUR: one spent in Chapter 0655, one spent in Chapter 0675, one due in Block 0005, and the fourth only if a chapter requires it.** The word *charter* and the word *custodian* are at 0 across the ten files, and the manuscript's word for a thing with no document is a thing with no name at the top of it, and a successor may name the thing if a card says so.

1594. **WHAT DID NOT MOVE, IN ONE LIST, AND EVERY ONE OF THEM IS STILL TRUE.** The bar of wood is in the grass at the foot of the lane by the Low Gate and nobody has lifted it and nobody is scheduled to lift it and the four questions on its board are not re-asked. **The line of stone on the bank at the market town's own gate is still in a line with the mallet on top of it, and no chapter of this block laid the fourth course, scheduled anybody laying it, or picked the mallet up.** The half leaf is face down and unscheduled and undescribed. **The register stands at ten questions with no finding under any of them, the fourth ruled head is empty and is not going to be filled, and the copyist has said in three months and in two rooms that she is not going to rule a fifth head.** The copyist's letter was not read out again and its promise is not revived. **The three series are three series and none is derived from another, the band figure of about a hundred and four on a fixed day has not changed and is not printed in this block, and about four hundred and nine parish rolls a month still come in.** The city is on a shelf with its three protected numerals at zero. **Nobody is thanked anywhere in this block, in any chapter, in any speech, and in any state file this block writes, and the thank family is 244 across the ten files, being 243 uses of *thanked* and one of *thank*, and every one of the 244 is a negation or a plain statement that a person was not thanked.** The road that went quiet in the middle of the nine days in Thawmonth is still quiet and is not named, visited, sent to or resolved. The shut road four hundred miles off is not reopened. The carter's mate of about twenty-two is not found. The coast's twenty-second notch is not cut and nobody was sent to the coast. **The two readings of a man who published his own mistake are not chosen between anywhere in this block.** The words *stage*, *ledger*, *qualification*, *charter*, *custodian* and *mechanism* are at 0 across the ten files, the whole *fail* family is at 0, and no System panel is printed.

1595. **THE MEASUREMENTS, RECOUNTED ON THE TEN FILES AS THEY STAND, WITH THE TWO BASES NAMED SEPARATELY.** **BASIS ONE, the file total: a plain whitespace split with the title line INCLUDED, each file counted on its own and the ten added. The block IS 36,958**, per chapter **3,805 / 3,760 / 3,595 / 3,666 / 3,881 / 3,852 / 3,431 / 3,429 / 3,597 / 3,942**, and a concatenated `cat` of the ten returns 36,958 and every file ends in a newline, so the two bases agree. **BASIS TWO, the scene body: everything above the paragraph that opens the cost coda, which therefore includes the title line and the dateline, so a body is not a scene body. The bodies ARE 3,548 / 3,474 / 3,276 / 3,426 / 3,652 / 3,606 / 3,101 / 3,144 / 3,316 / 3,682**, minimum 3,101 at `chapter-0687.md`. Codas are **257 / 286 / 319 / 240 / 229 / 246 / 330 / 285 / 281 / 260**, longest 330 at `chapter-0687.md`. **The mean is 3,696 a chapter.** `about` IS **23.85 to 29.17 per 1,000** over the ten, 1,020 in all, ceiling at `chapter-0681.md` at 29.17 and floor at `chapter-0690.md` at 23.85, **and five of the ten pages stand at or above 28.00, being 0681, 0685, 0686, 0687 and 0689.** **Zero em dashes, zero en dashes, zero semicolons, zero exclamation marks, zero single asterisks and therefore no italics, zero straight double quotes and zero curly apostrophes**, curly doubles balanced in every file, per-line bold parity even on every line, and **2 / 1 / 1 / 1 / 1 / 1 / 1 / 1 / 1 / 1 question marks, eleven in all, with no chapter at zero.** `Aren Kest` is in all ten. **Exact duplicate paragraphs at twelve words or more are 0 against the 680 prior files and 0 inside the block.** **The longest shared eight-word runs against the 680 prior files are 30, 25, 21, 20 and 17 windows, at `chapter-0682.md`, `chapter-0685.md`, `chapter-0684.md`, `chapter-0689.md` and `chapter-0687.md`, and all of them are house recurrences: the 30 is the copyist's own three-series sentence, which the block was required to state a third time in a third mouth; the 25 is the badge-man's formula for saying the true thing, and it runs to nine words because the second review restored it; the 21, the 20 and the 17 are the datelines of 0684, 0689 and 0687.** **A successor that re-measures should use `.get(window, <empty>)` and never `index[window]`, for the reason at `state/continuity.md` 1530. The longest shared run inside the block is 22 windows and is the dateline of `chapter-0682.md`.** **MONEY, with the extractor first and in the standing order. The scope is all 680 chapter files before this block, read whole and not sliced to the last two paragraphs. Extractor one, the printed total, clause-anchored on `is <spelled number> pence` and taken at a parsed value of 60 or above, returns 232 instances and 199 distinct. Extractor two, the restated total inside `and <spelled number> pence is <spelled number> marks and <spelled number> over`, returns 150 instances and 145 distinct, and under this extractor IS A STRICT SUBSET of extractor one. The union is 199 distinct. The four barred numerals appear nowhere in it, so the free set is 199 of 199. Column count: ten. Collision result: ZERO. 212 of the 680 prior files carry a printed total at or above 60 under this extractor.** The ten totals are **237, 252, 261, 277, 286, 295, 301, 313, 319 and 324**, ten distinct, ascending, each footing four ways with the conversion checked separately from the sum, ten items a column, ten distinct prices a column from 1 to 41, **ten distinct tails, at most eight values shared between any two columns, the lowest price in the block is 4, and no barred numeral is in any price, over-figure or printed total, and the over-figures are 45, 12, 21, 37, 46, 7, 13, 25, 31 and 36.** **THE THANK FAMILY IS 244, BEING 243 USES OF *thanked* AND ONE OF *thank*, AND EVERY ONE OF THE 244 IS A NEGATION OR A PLAIN STATEMENT THAT A PERSON WAS NOT THANKED, and the one bare *thank* is *did not thank him* in `chapter-0682.md`, and the same sweep returns the 28 that Volume 13 Block 0001's own summary claims, so the two blocks are counted on one basis.** The words *brother* and *sister* are at 0 across the ten files. **AND THE MANUSCRIPT IS 1,942,137 WORDS IN SIX HUNDRED AND NINETY CHAPTERS, Volumes 01 to 13 are 1,805,098 in six hundred and fifty and did not move, and Volume 14 is 137,039 in forty at a mean of 3,426, and its four blocks are 31,848, 34,987, 33,246 and 36,958, and the two volume figures add to the manuscript figure exactly.** The figure before this block was 1,905,179 in six hundred and eighty chapters, Volume 14 before this block was 100,081 in thirty, and Batch 0003 was 33,246; all three were recounted on the files this pass.
1596. **THE REVIEW REPAIR OF BATCH 0004, RECORDED BECAUSE A SUCCESSOR MUST INHERIT THE REPAIRS AND NOT THE DEFECTS, AND BECAUSE ONE OF THEM CHANGED WHAT A CHARACTER BELIEVES ON THE PAGE.** A reviewer read the ten files against the previous ten, the volume outline and the inherited hand-off, and found one error a reader can catch without a ledger and a number of smaller ones that were checked against the previous block's summaries rather than against the calendar and the previous block's pages. **Every finding that named a real contradiction is repaired and the repair is in the files; the findings that were matters of taste are not.** **ONE, and it was the worst thing in the block: in `chapter-0685.md` the man of about thirty-four with a satchel had said out loud in a market street that about four of the nine counties' asked people have been asked twice and that every one of the second visits has been refused and that being refused has not cost anybody anything anybody can put a price on, which the same page contradicted twice, because the woman of about forty-nine had said it herself in that street twenty minutes earlier and had lost a fortnight of a hired boy over it. The speech is now cut back to what he can actually stand on, that every one of the second visits was for a thing the person had not been asked before, and the woman of about forty-nine answers him in a hundred and three words, of which the first four stand by themselves: I was asked twice, and I refused the first and I did not refuse the second, and being refused is free, and being asked for something you have not been asked for is not free, and I am about forty-nine and I keep a kitchen. That exchange is now the best page in the chapter and it is the block's argument about asking, in the mouth of the person who paid for it.** **TWO, the nine tons of cut stone in `chapter-0681.md` were made to go past the market boards on a wain, which they cannot do, because they have not moved since the fourth of Fallowmonth and are still in the low approach to a crossing nine miles off; they are now reported where they are, and the third thing that goes past the boards is a sheet of printed paper.** **THREE, the chain at the ford was dated to the thirteenth of Fallowmonth in `chapter-0681.md` and `chapter-0682.md`; it has been on two posts there since the Sunday of the first week of Embermonth, which is the date `chapter-0675.md` gives, and both are corrected.** **FOUR, the man of about fifty-one's field is a mile and a half off the market town in five chapters and had become four miles in `chapter-0687.md`; every figure of four miles in that chapter is now a mile and a half, and his walk is a mile and a half.** **FIVE, the captain's elapsed time was five weeks in five places in `chapter-0689.md` and the eighth of Embermonth to the thirteenth of Frostmonth is sixty-five days, so all five are now nine weeks, and the ford is dated to the Sunday of the first week of Embermonth instead of this month.** **SIX, the crowd-stopping ordinals ran backwards in `chapter-0682.md`, with a fifth time before a fourth, and are now fourth and sixth, and the fourth in `chapter-0689.md` is now the third.** **SEVEN, three established people were doubled: the man who reads four clauses and points at one is the man of about forty-four who sells nails and is now named as him in `chapter-0681.md`; the wool seller two streets off is a man of about forty-seven and is not a woman of about thirty-three in `chapter-0684.md`; and the traveller with a basket and a stick is a man of about fifty-seven and is not a woman of about sixty in `chapter-0687.md`, which also removes a conflict with `chapter-0672.md`.** **EIGHT, `chapter-0682.md` merged the woman of about thirty-nine at a gate two days off with the woman of about fifty-nine who keeps the second gate from the top of the mile and a half, and restored the second; and her reference to the seventeenth of last month is now last month and not the month before last, because the speech is `chapter-0679.md` and that is Embermonth.** **NINE, the badge-man cannot have said in `chapter-0682.md` that he had not said it in that street for about four months when he had said it in that street five days earlier in `chapter-0681.md`, and the interval is now that he had said it once there five days earlier and in no market street before that.** **TEN, the kindness answer was restated in `chapter-0683.md`, which no chapter of this block may do, and the badge-man now says a different true thing about a shut room and nobody works out what he meant and he is not asked.** **ELEVEN, the nine-word counts attached to printed speeches were wrong in nine places, and every one of them has been counted off the printed words, so the badge-man's one-sentence formula in `chapter-0683.md`, `chapter-0684.md`, `chapter-0685.md`, `chapter-0687.md` and `chapter-0689.md` now carries its own figure, and where a claim could not be counted off the words printed beside it the claim is gone rather than wrong, which is what happened to the census line in `chapter-0683.md` and in `chapter-0688.md`.** THIS ITEM WAS HALF TRUE WHEN IT WAS WRITTEN AND THE SECOND REVIEW SAYS SO: it stated a policy that a count is dropped above about twenty words, and the block keeps a count at twenty-one in 0687 and at twenty-eight in 0689, and the policy now is only that every count is checkable against the speech printed beside it. **TWELVE, three durations did not foot: the hole under the market town's own gate has been widening since the middle of Harvestmonth and is about fifteen weeks, not eleven, in both places it appears; the man of about forty-three with a yard has been going over the nine tons of stone for about seventeen weeks; and the hired boy of about fourteen was hired in the second week of Rainmonth without anything in writing, and the renewal and the four and a half weeks are cut, so that the fortnight stands as the only figure.** **THIRTEEN, the rain stopped at two different hours in `chapter-0686.md` and now stops at about the fourth hour, and the lamp goes out at the sixth and the crew knock off at the seventh.** **FOURTEEN, the badge-man promised in `chapter-0684.md` to say the contractor's sentence twice in a market street and the second time was reported and never printed, and the sentence is now printed, word for word, in the man's own words, and that is the whole of what the badge-man said in that street that morning.** **FIFTEEN, the closing sequence of `chapter-0690.md` had a man of about sixty-two walking down the made road with three horses and simultaneously being a quarter of a mile ahead of a column going up it, and he is now on the wain going up.** **SIXTEEN, two thank-family occurrences in `chapter-0688.md` were not negations, a lodging-house keeper thanking a book and a man thanking a door by not thanking it, and both are now a woman saying something kind about a book and a man valuing a door he had not been thanked for, which brings the block's thank family to 230 and all 230 are negations or plain statements that a person was not thanked.** **SEVENTEEN, the meta voice had taken over the block's closing chapter, and nine occurrences of the words volume and block across `chapter-0690.md` are now in-world, and the one in `chapter-0686.md` and the one in `chapter-0688.md` are as well.** **EIGHTEEN, the second cost was announced in the narration as being said in a fourth mouth in `chapter-0687.md`, which the hand-off does not allow and which the narration should not have said anyway, and the announcement is cut.** **NINETEEN, the roster of nine in `chapter-0687.md` did not match its own printed definition, and the first sentence of the speech has been re-cut so that the nine are people who have been asked something in the last year and have gone and said no at a gate, which is what all nine of them have done.** **TWENTY, the rule the contractor says the badge-man put in this county is dated a year and a half, which is what `chapter-0655.md` gives, and not four and a half, which is the badge-man's street-standing figure; the six clerks are the clerks, because the hearing has two; and the stick in `chapter-0683.md` is in the right hand, as it is in the other nine.** **AND TWO FINDINGS WERE NOT REPAIRED AND ARE FLAGGED FOR THE SUCCESSOR.** **TWENTY-ONE, the census sentence is at a density in this block where it has stopped recording perception and become punctuation, and about one paragraph in three in `chapter-0686.md` and one in two in the others is a count of who understood and who did not. It is the house's own construction and it is load-bearing, and this block ran it harder than Batch 0003 did, and the standing advice to Batch 0005 is to keep it for the two or three beats in a chapter where who understood is the point and to write the rest as action, weather or silence.** **TWENTY-TWO, one conflict between two inherited instructions was not on the page and is now recorded: the hand-off at `state/open-threads.md` 582 forbids turning one person saying no at one gate into a practice, a rule or a name, and `outline/volume-14.md` §8 Block 4 and the hand-off at 591 make the coalition without a charter this block's subject, and `chapter-0687.md` names nine people aloud in a market street by their trades. The reading taken is that naming the nine by their trades is not turning one gate into a practice, because the chapter does not establish that they have ever acted together, does not give them a name, does not put them in any series, and the last thing the chapter does with the list is refuse to write it down. That reading is recorded here so that Batch 0005 inherits one instruction and not two, and Batch 0005 may not build on the list as though it were a roster.**

1597. **THE SECOND REVIEW OF BATCH 0004, RECORDED BECAUSE THE FIRST REVIEW CORRECTED THE PAGES AND NOT THE LAYER ABOVE THEM, AND BECAUSE A SUCCESSOR THAT INHERITS THE FIRST FIGURES WILL WRITE THE SAME MISTAKES.** A second reviewer read the ten files against the previous ten and against this ledger and found that **every contradiction named in 1596 is gone from the files and that the damage had moved one level up, into the numbers printed about the files and into four counts printed on the pages that 1596 claimed to have recounted.** Nine stale figures and eight prose defects were named and **every one of them was checked on the files before anything was changed, and every one of them was real.** **THE PROSE, in the order of the pages. `chapter-0688.md` printed *the reason in four words* against a printed speech of six, and it is six, and the census line under it had been given *twelve words* and *three months* for a fifty-three word speech in two sentences, and both are now gone from the line rather than replaced by another unverifiable figure, and the narration above it said *three sentences* of a speech that has two and now says two. `chapter-0683.md` printed *the same twelve words* against a sixty-three word speech and now says the fourth head was argued out in her own trade. `chapter-0685.md` credited the woman of about forty-nine with *thirty-two words* when the thirty-two words are the livery man's line that comes after her, and her own speech is a hundred and three words, which is what the census now says. `chapter-0689.md` printed *28 words* in Arabic, which was the only Arabic numeral in a word count in the whole block, and it is twenty-eight. `chapter-0687.md` had been given *two months* where `chapter-0681.md`, `chapter-0684.md` and `chapter-0689.md` all have the same man waiting *about four months*, and the four months are back, because the second review took the view that a recurrence is worth more than a variation invented by a repair.** **ELEVEN printed figures now stand on the ten pages and every one of them counts the words printed beside it, which is eleven claims and eleven correct counts, against the nine claims of 1596 of which four were wrong.** **THE HOUSE FORMULA, AND THIS IS THE ONE FINDING THAT WAS NOT A MATTER OF TASTE. The phrase *nine words* stood at sixty-six in Chapters 0661 to 0680 and at ZERO in Chapters 0681 to 0680's successor 0690, because 1596's repair replaced the figure on every badge-man speech with its true count and, in doing so, retired the manuscript's most repeated construction.** `workspace/volume-14/batch-0005/PROMPT.md` asks for that formula by name in the Chapter 0691 card, and the writer of that block reads this block for voice, so the loss was not cosmetic. **It is restored where the block's own census had already paraphrased it.** The census under the badge-man's line in `chapter-0685.md` says the thing this county built is not the difficulty and that the asking is, which did not match a speech that read *the rule is not what is taking them*; the speech is now **The rule is not the difficulty. The asking is.** and it is nine words and the narration says nine words, and the paraphrase beside it is exact. **No other speech in the block was re-cut to reach a figure, and the other per-speech counts stand at eighteen, four, twelve, sixteen, twelve, seventeen, twenty-one, six, fourteen and twenty-eight.** **THE STATE, and every figure below was recounted on the files after the prose was changed.** **The block is 36,958, not 36,955. The manuscript is 1,942,137, not 1,942,134. Volume 14 is 137,039, not 137,036, and its mean is 3,426 and not 3,418. The block's mean is 3,696 and not 3,663. The shortest page is 3,429 at `chapter-0688.md` and the second longest is 3,881 at `chapter-0685.md`, and 1595 and the summaries had both said 3,414 and 3,833, which are two figures for pages that do not exist at those lengths. The `about` band is 23.85 to 29.17 and five of the ten pages are at or above 28.00. The thank family is 244 and three files printed the self-contradictory pair of 230 and 228. The eight-word runs are 30, 25, 21, 20 and 17 and 1595 explained a run of 26 that was not in its own list. The longest coda is 330 at `chapter-0687.md` and the summaries called 319 at `chapter-0683.md` the longest. Seven of the ten chapter headings in `state/chapter-summaries.md` carried a stale word count.** **THE LESSON, AND IT IS THE THING A SUCCESSOR SHOULD CARRY: 1596 verified the pages against the calendar and against the previous block's summaries, and it did not verify its own arithmetic, so a correction made to a page moved three numbers that were printed in four files and never moved back. The instrument that would have caught all of it is the cheapest one in this manuscript and it is to count the files again after the last prose edit and then grep the state files for every figure the recount changed, before writing a single word of the repair into a ledger.** **The three figures a successor may inherit without recounting are 36,958 for the block, 137,039 for Volume 14 and 1,942,137 for the manuscript, and the three it must recount are the calendar, the money collision set, and the age of the protagonist, which was still flagged and still not acted on when this was written. THE FIRST TWO OF THOSE THREE FIGURES HAVE SINCE MOVED AND A SUCCESSOR MUST NOT INHERIT THEM: 1,942,137 and 137,039 were superseded by the close of Volume 14 and are 1,977,530 and 172,432, while 36,958 is Batch 0004's exact total and is still correct and is not superseded. The age of the protagonist is resolved at `outline/volumes/volume-14-close.md` section 9 and is thirty-two at the close of Volume 14.**

## Volume 14 Batch 0005, the block that closed the volume, Chapters 691 to 700

1598. **THE BLOCK'S FRAME, AND IT IS DERIVED AND NOT COPIED.** Day 930, the twenty-fifth of Mudmonth YR 318, a Sunday, is the only anchor, on the carried thirty-day month. **Block 0005 is days 2257 to 2310 inclusive, fifty-four days, and every day of it is YR 321 because the year does not turn until day 2341.** Hearthmonth the first is day 2256 and is a Wednesday. **Goatmonth the first is day 2286, a Friday, not a market morning, and it is the only month start inside the block.** Market mornings are the Saturdays and the Thursdays. **The ten days of the block as written are 2257, 2260, 2266, 2269, 2273, 2277, 2280, 2281, 2286 and 2310. Four are market mornings and six are not. One is the first of a month.** The six that are not carry the room over the weigh-house shut behind its own door in the dateline on the page. **The printed table at `outline/volume-13.md` §17 was not read for any day of this block and is wrong in five rows, of which day 2256, the day before this block, is the fifth.** All fifty datelines of Volume 14 reconcile on the one anchor.

1599. **THE THING WITH NO HEAD WON, AND IT WON BY NAMING NOTHING, IN FULL.** Nine days of held crossings from the second of Hearthmonth to the tenth inclusive. **Four gates were standing on the second and nine on the ninth, and the count exists because four people counted and five heard and nobody made a total of the total.** The procedure is nine words said out loud once in a market street and not said again that day, and it is that nobody says anything to anybody and nobody is asked. **It is not a muster, not a list, not a watch, not a rota, and there is not a name at the top of it and there is not going to be one.** Nobody in nine counties named a road to the captain of about forty-four in fifty chapters, and the column did not take the ferry city and did not take the works and did not take a gatehouse. **The badge-man said in Chapter 0691 that the arrangement cannot report whether it worked and that on the tenth there will be nine gates or there will be four and about nine people will be able to tell you which, and that this is the whole of the reporting and there is no more of it.**

1600. **THE PRICE OF IT, NAMED OUT LOUD BY A PERSON WHO IS NOT THE PROTAGONIST, IN FULL.** **Nell Drury, thirty-three, who keeps a chandler's shop at the low end of the boards, is the sixth and last name in Volume 14's budget of six, and she is spent in this block, and she cannot be a person without one.** In Chapter 0691 she says that about four hundred people are going to walk out of the market street and about nine of them are going to know that four gates in nine counties are standing shut on somebody's account, and about three hundred and ninety of the rest are going to walk past a thing they cannot name, and **that this is the arrangement working and that this is exactly what four and a half years of it is for.** She says not one person in that street is brave. She also refuses a book, because a shopkeeper who writes nine gates down is a shopkeeper a man can come to and ask which gate. **In Chapter 0699 she puts a cloth over a bench she has put out of her shop every market morning for eleven years with nothing on it, and that is the last beat of the volume's resolution and it is small and local and not the protagonist's, and nobody has asked her what is under it.**

1601. **THE NINE MINUTES, IN FULL, AND NOBODY HAS STANDING TO SAY HE WAS RIGHT.** Chapter 0695, Hearthmonth the eighteenth, a market morning, sleet off the water. **Aren Kest went down on his knees on a wet plank deck with a bad leg, put his right hand under a cracked sill beam and worked it back and forth a quarter of an inch at a time, and the whole of it came in about nine minutes and nobody was hurt and nobody was under it.** The prop in that bridge has been there since the middle of Longlight. **The man of about thirty-eight who keeps that gate said nine words, which are that nothing holds up when you are not on it. The man of about forty-four who keeps a wain is what actually stopped the cart: he turned his team off a one-cart lane up about nine feet of wet bank and backed the wain down across the lane so its tail stood square across it, and a cart cannot get round a wain and cannot get past one.** **The badge-man came at the fourth hour, stood at the low end in the wet, did not go on the bridge, and said in thirty-eight words that he has no clause for it and has had none since the middle of Longlight and is not going to write one at a quarter past eleven on a wet Saturday at a gate. He did not say one word about who did it and nobody asked him.** He did not thank him and did not defend him and did not speak for him. **The nine men of that gate went on and worked out all eleven days of it at a crossing that was not passable and four of them carried the barrow over the low side on a rope, and nobody asked them for that figure and it cannot be handed back to anybody.** The word *failed* is not used about anything at all in this chapter or in this volume.

1602. **THE HOLES IN THE ARRANGEMENT ARE THREE AND THEN FOUR, IN THE ORDER THE EVENTS HAPPEN, IN FULL.** Hole one, the standing one: the three clauses cover roads that can be written to. Hole two: the three clauses are about a man doing a thing to a road, and a paper does not do a thing to a road. **Hole three is a figure, and it is Chapter 0694: the badge-man says in twenty-four words that they have sent a number and that he has three clauses about people and roads and not one of them is about a number. Hole four is the bridge, and it is Chapter 0695: a rule about roads being shut is a rule about the man who proposes the shutting, and nobody proposed this and there is no clause about a man who does not propose.** **The ordinals were wrong in the first pass, where Chapter 0694 called its hole the fourth and Chapter 0695 called the bridge the third, and they now run third then fourth and the repair is in the files. Neither hole is called small and neither is resolved. A successor may not renumber them and may not call either one small.**

1603. **THE TWO REFUSALS, THE KIN-TERM COLLISION, AND HOW IT WAS RESOLVED, IN FULL.** Chapter 0696, Hearthmonth the twenty-second, a Wednesday, about nine people in a market street with no market in it, four days after the bridge went down. **`Tovan Kest`, a carrier of about twenty-eight, has come four days on foot on his own account. He is the protagonist's near kin. He refused, out loud and in the open, to let a carter name the carriers, and gave the reason in his own trade, which is that a path community is nine houses and a set of crossings with no head, that nobody is told where to be, and that nobody up that road had a reason to be at that crossing on the second hour of the eighteenth. THEN HE REFUSED THE HARDER THING, which is that nobody will name the man who let the bridge go, and he did it by reading out a leaf dated the eleventh of Hearthmonth on which nine houses at a place called Nine Elms wrote that the roads on that coast shut nothing, two of them signing and seven thumbing, and putting the date beside it, and saying that a deck is a thing anybody can look at and a man is not. About four people in that street understand the second refusal and about five only understand the first. A carter's book gets one line in it on that Wednesday that says nobody told me and the date and nothing else. There is a second leaf dated the ninth which is not read out in that street and has not been read out anywhere and is not addressed to anybody. Aren Kest stood at the high end of those boards through the whole of it with his stick in his right hand and did not speak once and nobody looked at him.** **THE KIN-TERM IS PRINTED NOWHERE IN THIS CHAPTER AND NOWHERE IN THIS VOLUME, AND THE RELATIONSHIP IS THE SHAPE OF THE ARGUMENT AND NOT A STATED FACT.** See `outline/volumes/volume-14-close.md` section 10.

1604. **THE GATEHOUSE IS EXPOSED AND THE MECHANISM IS NOT EXPLAINED, IN FULL.** Chapter 0697, Hearthmonth the twenty-fifth, about four hundred people in that market street and about two hundred of them stopped, the largest crowd in that market town since the first of Embermonth. **Four conversations went in it four feet apart at about the second hour and nobody put them together on purpose.** A cloth seller of about thirty-three stopped at the ninth mile stone because a man in a livery walked about nine paces up to a wall and stood against it with his back to the road. A wain driver of about forty-four has driven that made road since he was about nine and has never once been through the gate in that wall and would not go through it if it was open. A beekeeper of about nineteen has seen smoke off a chimney behind that wall four days in seven since he was about eleven and has never asked what is behind it. A yard keeper of about forty-three had a cart of his own go in there about nine years ago and come out loaded and was told not to ask and has thought about not asking once a week for about nine years. **About nine people in nine counties put four conversations together and got to about four and a half years.** What is exposed is not a road and not a bridge and not a column: it is that there is a Gatehouse, that it can be seen from a made road, and that about nine people in nine counties now know where it is. **No mechanism is explained, no character says what a roadmark is, the notebook's separate leaf that says *a person* is not looked at, the words *stage*, *ledger* and *qualification* are at 0 across the chapter, and NO SYSTEM PANEL IS SPENT IN THIS CHAPTER OR ANYWHERE IN THIS VOLUME.**

1605. **THE PRACTICAL LIMIT OF THE STAGE IS A PERSON, IN FULL.** Chapter 0698, Hearthmonth the twenty-sixth, a Sunday, not a market morning. **Aren Kest has gone out on that made road every morning since the first of Longlight. There are three occasions. At the sixth mile stone on the nineteenth he could not take his hand off the edging of stone. At the seventh mile on the twenty-first, where the high side stops being a bank and becomes a wall-foot, he got his hand on the knee-high stone and could not go past it. On the twenty-sixth, at about two hundred yards short of the shut gate, his left foot would not go forward, and he put it forward twice, waited, tried a third time, and did not try a fourth, because a man who tries a thing nine times is a man who wants it to work, and he put his stick down in the wet grass in order to try to walk two hundred yards.** He walked back and did not go out again that day. **Nobody in nine counties was angry with him and nobody asked him about it, and about nine people in that market town knew and about nine counties were not told and have not been told since.** The woman of about fifty-one who keeps a lodging house comes down a lane and says the true thing about a man who cannot come and it is not comfort: a bed is for a person who has come off a road, there is not a bed for a person who has not been able to come off a road, and those are two different people, and there is nothing in her trade that fills a bed because a man does not go. **On the Monday he goes up that road at about the ninth hour as he has every morning since the first of Longlight. This is the page where the cap of four and a half years of a man being the one who goes stops, and nobody in nine counties has been told that it stops.**

1606. **THE HEARING, THE RESOLUTION, AND THE MARCH, IN FULL.** Chapter 0699, Goatmonth the first, a Friday, not a market morning, the first of a month. **About nine people came to boards that stand in an empty street and about five did not, and they were not the nine who came on the first of Hearthmonth. The badge-man said nothing for two hours, which nine people in nine counties say is the hardest thing he has ever done, and four people came up to him one at a time and asked him things and he answered nothing and nobody left and nobody wrote anything down.** He then said nine words, that nothing happens on the first of the next month, and ten words, that nobody can call the next meeting and nobody needs to, and **the reason there is nothing at the top of it is not that nobody wanted to put something there, it is that nobody in nine counties could call the next meeting and nobody needs to, because the thing that wanted a meeting was a man with a warrant and he has stopped.** The hearing certifies nothing in fourteen words and the register stands at ten questions with no finding under any of them and the fourth ruled head empty for about eleven months and no fifth head ruled and the day-book at eleven entries. **The march came into nine counties and went out of it again and did not take one road and did not take the ferry city and did not take the works, and not one person in nine counties has ever said the word for what that is. The word *failed* is not used about anything at all in this volume.** **The exposure of the works is worse for everybody and no chapter says it is better: about four people in nine counties have said out loud since the twenty-fifth of Hearthmonth that they would give the whole of the last four and a half years back to have the wall shut again, and about five have said exactly the same thing, and neither set has ever spoken to the other.**

1607. **WHO IS NOT IN THE STREET AND WHO WAS NOT ASKED, IN FULL.** On the first of Goatmonth: **the captain of about forty-four is on a made road four days off going south in the wet, with a list of about thirty counties in order that went up the made road in a handbasket on the Saturday after he turned and that is in the hired room over a saddler's under a cloth on the same table as nine hundred words nobody has withdrawn, and about nine of the thirty are struck through with a single line each and about twenty-one counties are still on the list with nothing against them and nine of those have not been read since the eighth of Embermonth. The woman of about thirty-nine who keeps the second gate from the top of the made road is at her gate and her gate is open and she is not in that street and nobody sent for her and nobody sent anybody to her. The delegation got home. The city four hundred miles off has not been asked a question by anybody in nine counties and is not in that street and nobody is being sent to it.** **A successor may not put any of these four in that street and may not have anybody ask the city anything and may not send anybody to the coast.**

1608. **THE ONE PRINTING OF THE ANTAGONIST'S NAME IN THIS BLOCK, IN FULL.** **Chapter 0694, Hearthmonth the fourteenth. The man of about thirty-one in a livery turns a lawful sealed sheet over at a gate and reads the hand at the top of it, which is what a man does when he reads a paper in the open, and he reads it the way he reads everything, which is that he reads what was on the paper. The two words are: “And by the Marshal's hand. Edric Hunn.”** The volume cap is FOUR and the printings are Chapter 0655, Chapter 0675 and Chapter 0694, and the fourth was not required and was not spent. **Every other reference in this block is *that name* or *the Marshal's hand*, and the name arrives as a document and not as a man.** Nothing about the non-naming in Volumes 03 to 13 is undone and no beat is moved.

1609. **THE NAME BUDGET, COUNTED ON FIRST PRINTINGS, AND IT CLOSED AT FOUR OF SIX AND NOT SIX OF SIX.** The budget for Volume 14 is six and the whole volume may spend six. **A name is spent when it is first printed in this volume, and a person who was named in an earlier volume and is printed here again is a return and not a spend, which is the standing convention at 1538 and 1555.** **Block 0001 spent two, `Salla Dunnock` at `chapter-0652.md` and `Halvard Rees` at `chapter-0660.md`. Block 0002 spent one, `Maud Trenchard` at `chapter-0663.md`. Blocks 0003 and 0004 spent none. Block 0005 spent one, `Nell Drury` at `chapter-0697.md`. THE VOLUME CLOSED AT FOUR OF SIX AND TWO SLOTS WERE UNSPENT.** `Nell Drury` is the only spend of this block, the prompt for it reserved the last name for a person who cannot be a person without one, and she is a shopkeeper who prices the arrangement in a market street and who is the last beat of the volume's resolution; **she is named once in the volume and once in this block, in Chapter 0697, and is the woman of about thirty-three who keeps a chandler's shop in Chapters 0691 and 0699 and not named in either.** **`Edric Hunn` IS NOT A SPEND OF THIS BUDGET: he is named in `chapters/volume-01/chapter-0026.md` as *Edric Hunn, marshal*, so he is an established return, he is not in `outline/volume-14.md` §13's cast table, and his three printings in this volume at Chapters 0655, 0675 and 0694 are debited to the antagonist's own cap of four at §16 item 4 and not to this one; a batch phase entered him as a spend of this block, which made two budgets carry one name.** `Mara Vey`, `Hester Vane`, `Sera Quen` and `Tovan Kest` are returns and are not spends; **`outline/volume-14.md` §13 lists `Mara Vey` among the volume's six and 1538 records her as a return, and that disagreement is recorded at `outline/volumes/volume-14-close.md` §6 and is not settled here, and under the outline's reading the volume is five of six.** `Tovan Kest` is named in this volume from `chapter-0654.md` and not first in Chapter 0696, which is where he refuses twice. `Sabra Rolf` and `Gallow` were not used for a new first name. **A capitalised two-word sweep over this block's ten files returns only established names, the one spend of this block, and the name of a place.**

1610. **THE KIN-TERM IS AT ZERO ACROSS ALL FIFTY CHAPTERS OF VOLUME 14, AND THE ONE COLLISION IS RESOLVED.** `chapter-0654.md` spends the volume's cap of four and the word is not printed again in any of the fifty chapters. **`outline/volume-14.md` §16 item 5 records that the kin-term returns in this volume under a cap of four and that the first of the four is Chapter 0654, while `outline/series.md`'s Volume 14 relationship row requires Tovan Kest to refuse publicly, and §16 item 5 itself says a public refusal of that kind cannot be made by two men who have never used the word for each other in prose. THE TWO INSTRUCTIONS COLLIDE AND THE COLLISION WAS RESOLVED ON THE PAGE AND NOT BY PRINTING THE WORD: the two refusals in Chapter 0696 are made out loud and in the open in a street with about nine people in it, the second is the harder, it is made by reading out a dated leaf rather than by naming a man, and the crowd reads the two men as two people who have a reason to protect each other. The relationship is the shape of the argument and not a stated fact. Recorded in full at `outline/volumes/volume-14-close.md` section 10 and at item 1603 here.**

1611. **THE SEPARATION IS OPEN AND WAS NOT MENDED IN THIS BLOCK AND COULD NOT BE.** The four hundred paces and the two doors stand. **There is no exchange of words between Aren Kest and the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps the room in any of this block's ten files.** He stood at the high end of the boards in Chapter 0696 and did not speak and was not looked at. In Chapter 0698 he stood at his own window and looked down four hundred paces at a shut room with a door on a lane and did not go down the lane. In Chapter 0700 he walked past that lane four hundred paces from a door he has gone past since the first of Longlight and did not go up it, and nobody has ever asked him to and nobody in nine counties has said to him that he was not going to. **Nothing between them is in any document, the four and a half years are never referred to as four and a half years about the two of them, and no chapter of this volume mends anything.** `outline/volume-14.md` §10 item 5 requires the two of them to work together and keep the argument, and that is compatible with the four hundred paces, and this block put them in one street with about nine people in it and did not mend anything.

1612. **VOLUME 15 IS SET UP ON THE PAGE IN THIS VOLUME'S OWN TERMS, IN FULL, AS `outline/volume-14.md` §11 REQUIRES.** **One, a person who knows where the oldest path is and has said nothing about it for about eleven years: the man of about forty-one who keeps a drove and a byre and about nine hundred yards of wall at the head of the made road, who in Chapter 0700 is asked one question out loud at a gate, which is how old is that lane, and answers in ten words, which are that it was a road before this county had a name, and who has known it for about eleven years and about nine people in nine counties knew that he knew it before he said it and about five had never once thought of asking him. Two, a Gatehouse that is now exposed and can therefore be looked at: a wall about nine feet high with stone coping on a made road at nine miles, with a gate in it about four feet wide and no latch on the outside, a roof behind it and a chimney four days in seven. Three, a refusal that two path communities have already given in writing: the leaf of the eleventh of Hearthmonth that nine houses wrote at Nine Elms, and the second leaf dated the ninth that is carried in the inside of a coat, has not been read out anywhere, and is not addressed to anybody.** A successor may not produce route notes, may not quote them, may not mention them, and may not have any character know that they exist.

1613. **THE MEASUREMENTS OF BLOCK 0005, RECOUNTED ON THE TEN FILES AS THEY STAND, WITH THE TWO BASES NAMED SEPARATELY.** **BASIS ONE, the file total: a plain whitespace split with the title line INCLUDED, each file counted on its own and the ten added. The block IS 35,393**, per chapter **3,909 / 3,478 / 3,621 / 3,517 / 3,861 / 3,503 / 3,663 / 3,471 / 3,669 / 2,701**, and every file ends in a newline. **BASIS TWO, the scene body: everything above the paragraph that opens the cost coda, which therefore includes the title line and the dateline, so a body is not a scene body. The bodies ARE 3,661 / 3,245 / 3,352 / 3,278 / 3,613 / 3,231 / 3,430 / 3,254 / 3,419 / 2,435**, minimum 2,435 at `chapter-0700.md`, which is the volume's last page and is meant to be short. Codas **248 / 233 / 269 / 239 / 248 / 272 / 233 / 217 / 250 / 266**, minimum 217, maximum 272. **The block mean is 3,539.** `about` IS **23.00 to 27.69 per 1,000**, 908 in all, floor at `chapter-0692.md` and ceiling at `chapter-0696.md`, **and no page is at or above 28.00**, which is the standing advice at 1596 finding twenty-one. **Zero em dashes, zero en dashes, zero semicolons, zero exclamation marks, zero single asterisks and therefore no italics, zero straight double quotes and zero curly apostrophes**, straight apostrophes only, curly doubles balanced in every file, per-line bold parity even on every line, and **2 / 2 / 1 / 1 / 1 / 1 / 1 / 1 / 1 / 1 question marks, twelve in all, with no chapter at zero.** **Exact duplicate paragraphs at twelve words or more are 0 against the 690 prior files and 0 inside the block.** **The longest shared eight-word runs against the 690 prior files are 28, 20, 18, 17 and 14 windows, at `chapter-0691.md`, `chapter-0699.md`, `chapter-0697.md`, `chapter-0692.md` and `chapter-0693.md`, where the fourteen is tied with `chapter-0700.md`, and every one of them is a house recurrence: an established character described in the house's own formulae, the dateline frame, and the cost-coda opening.** **A successor that re-measures should use `.get(window, <empty>)` and never `index[window]`, for the reason at `state/continuity.md` 1530.** **THE THANK FAMILY IS 229 ACROSS THE TEN FILES, BEING 226 USES OF *thanked* AND THREE OF *thank*, AND EVERY ONE OF THE 229 IS A NEGATION OR A PLAIN STATEMENT THAT A PERSON WAS NOT THANKED.**

1614. **THE MONEY OF BLOCK 0005, FOOTED FOUR WAYS, WITH THE EXTRACTOR FIRST AND IN THE STANDING ORDER.** **Extractor one: the printed total, clause-anchored on `is <spelled number> pence`, taken at a parsed value of 60 or above. Extractor two: the restated total inside `and <spelled number> pence is <spelled number> marks and <spelled number> over`, and under this extractor it IS A STRICT SUBSET of extractor one. Scope: all 690 chapter files before this block, read whole and not sliced to the last two paragraphs. Instances: 330. Distinct: 262. Extractor two distinct: 189. Union: 262. Free set: 262 of 262, and the four barred numerals appear nowhere in it. Column count: ten. Collision result: ZERO.** The ten totals are **180, 200, 242, 258, 263, 270, 293, 299, 316 and 331 pence**, ten distinct, ascending, ten items a column, ten distinct prices a column from 1 to 41, **ten distinct tails, so no two columns end on the same two items at the same two prices**, the lowest price in the block is 1, and **no barred numeral is in any price, over-figure or printed total**, and the over-figures are 36, 8, 2, 18, 23, 30, 5, 11, 28 and 43. **EVERY COLUMN'S PRINTED WORDS PARSE TO ITS OWN SUM AND EVERY COLUMN'S MARKS AND OVER-FIGURE MULTIPLY BACK OUT AT FORTY-EIGHT PENCE A MARK, AND THE CONVERSION WAS CHECKED SEPARATELY FROM THE SUM.** **AND ONE COLUMN WAS RE-CUT IN THIS PASS: `chapter-0691.md` stood at one hundred and eighty-six pence, which is a printed total at `chapters/volume-11/chapter-0548.md`, so under the declared extractor it was a collision with a volume eleven columns back; the sixth item in that column, a link of chain, was moved from twenty-one to fifteen and the total is now one hundred and eighty pence, which is free, and the marks conversion was re-footed at three marks and thirty-six over.** A collision set is a set of numbers plus the extractor that built it, and the seven figures above are printed so that a successor can rebuild the set rather than inherit it.

1615. **THE REPAIRS OF BLOCK 0005, RECORDED BECAUSE A SUCCESSOR MUST INHERIT THE REPAIRS AND NOT THE DEFECTS.** **ONE, the money collision at one hundred and eighty-six, repaired as recorded at 1614. TWO, `chapter-0691.md` said the room over the weigh-house had been shut since noon on the Saturday and then said it was open on the same morning in the same sentence; the standing rule is that the room is shut on every day that is not a market morning and that it shuts at noon on a Saturday until the seventh hour on a Monday, and 0691 is a Thursday, and it now says the rule and its own day. THREE, `chapter-0697.md` shut the room on the ground that a Saturday is not a market morning, on a page whose own dateline says it is a Saturday and a market morning, and the second hour is before noon; it now gives the noon rule. FOUR, `chapter-0694.md` said the room is shut about two days in seven where the standing figure is five; it now says five. FIVE, `chapter-0694.md` called its hole the fourth one and `chapter-0695.md`, four days later, called the bridge the third; the ordinals now run third then fourth, which is the order the events happen in. SIX, every count attached to a printed speech in the ten files was counted off the words printed beside it, and eleven such claims are in the block and all eleven are correct. AND ONE FINDING IS FLAGGED AND NOT REPAIRED: the census sentence, which counts who understood and who did not, is still at a high density in Chapters 0691, 0693, 0696 and 0697, and the standing advice at 1596 finding twenty-one was to keep it for the two or three beats in a chapter where who understood is the point and write the rest as action, weather or silence. The `about` density is at 23.00 to 27.69 and no page is at or above 28.00, which is that advice taken, and the census is still the block's most conspicuous construction and a successor should treat it as load-bearing and rate it against the page rather than cut it.**

1616. **THE MANUSCRIPT AFTER VOLUME 14, RECOUNTED ON THE SEVEN HUNDRED FILES.** **1,977,530 words in seven hundred chapters.** Volumes 01 to 13 are 1,805,098 in six hundred and fifty and did not move by one word, because nothing outside `chapters/volume-14/`, the state files and `outline/volumes/volume-14-close.md` was touched. **Volume 14 is 172,432 in fifty chapters at a mean of 3,449, and its five blocks are 31,848, 34,987, 33,246, 36,958 and 35,393, and the two volume figures add to the manuscript figure exactly: 1,805,098 + 172,432 = 1,977,530.** **THE FIGURES 1,942,137 AND 137,039 WERE THE PRE-BLOCK FIGURES AND ARE NOW SUPERSEDED IN EVERY LIVE FILE THAT CARRIED THEM; the correct figures are 1,977,530 and 172,432. 36,958 IS NOT SUPERSEDED AND IS NOT STALE: it is Batch 0004's exact total, it did not move, and this block's 35,393 is a figure about Chapters 691 to 700 and not a correction of a figure about Chapters 681 to 690, so a successor finding 36,958 in a live file is reading Batch 0004's figure and not a wrong one.** **VOLUME 14 IS CLOSED.** The close record is `outline/volumes/volume-14-close.md` and it carries the volume index, the block means, the two counting bases, the ten datelines and the calendar, the two figures a reader would otherwise take on trust, the one naming decision and the one printing of the antagonist's name in this block, the name budget at four of six with two slots unspent, and the two findings a volume close is the right place for.

## The review repair of the Volume 14 close, item 1617, and the highest number in this file is now 1617

1617. **A REVIEW RAN OVER THE CLOSING PHASE AND FOUND THIRTEEN DEFECTS IN ITS OWN CERTIFICATES AND NONE IN ITS CHAPTERS, AND EVERY ONE IS REPAIRED IN THE FILES. NO CHAPTER OF VOLUME 14 WAS TOUCHED BY THIS PASS, THE WORD COUNTS DID NOT MOVE, AND NOTHING WAS ADDED TO ANY PAGE.** A reviewer read `outline/volumes/volume-14-close.md` and the four state ledgers against the seven hundred chapter files and returned the findings below; every figure in this item was recounted on the files before anything was changed and every one of them was real. **The finding is the one this ledger has recorded three times already and it is still the finding: a repair made to a page, or a count made about a repair, moved numbers that were printed about the files, and the layer above the pages is where they went.**

**THE NINE FINDINGS THE REVIEW NAMED. ONE, the close record and `state/current.md` called 36,958 stale with the other two superseded figures and offered this block's 35,393 as its correction, which is wrong: 36,958 is Batch 0004's exact total, it did not move, and the batch prompt for this block had asked for *the two figures a reader would otherwise take on trust*, which is two. Corrected in the close record, in `state/current.md`, in `state/batch-summaries.md` and at 1616, and 36,958 is now named as a correct figure in all four. TWO, the close record described the 0691 money repair as *the tenth item's price moved from forty to thirty-four* when the moved item is the sixth, a link of chain, from twenty-one to fifteen; corrected in the close record and in `state/batch-summaries.md`. THREE, the close record said FIVE names were spent before this block against an itemisation of four, which is seven against a cap of six, and `Edric Hunn` was debited to two budgets without a note; see 1609 and the close record's §6. FOUR, `state/current.md` said the holes in the arrangement were now three on the line before naming hole three and hole four. FIVE, `state/open-threads.md` 618 put the man of about forty-one in Chapter 0690, where `chapter-0690.md` has no instance of *forty-one*, *drove*, *byre* or *five-bar*, and Chapter 0700 has all four. SIX, `Nell Drury` was credited to Chapters 0691 and 0699, where she is not named, and is named once, in Chapter 0697. SEVEN, four measurement errors in one paragraph: thirteen question marks where the files hold twelve, a thank family of 227 and two where it is 226 and three, a coda share of 6.84 per cent where 2,475 of 35,393 is 6.99, and 909 uses of `about` where there are 908. EIGHT, the close record said four datelines of Volume 14 stood before this block and forty-nine others now on disk, which is fifty-three in a volume of fifty; forty stood before it and ten are in it. NINE, the retired-prompt note was headed Two and listed three, named a `workspace/volume-11/batch-0001/` that does not exist, and said the two that do exist carry no `.done` without saying they carry a `.retired`. All nine corrected in the files named beside them.**

**AND FOUR MORE THIS PASS FOUND ON ITS OWN RECOUNT, WHICH ARE THE SAME FOUR CLASSES AGAIN.** TEN, the close record called the block's minimum body 2,435 *the volume's own floor*, and `chapter-0653.md` is 2,304 and is the floor. ELEVEN, the shared eight-word runs were quoted as 28, 20, 18, 17 and 14 windows, which are the right five figures, and were paired with the wrong five chapters; they are `chapter-0691.md`, `chapter-0699.md`, `chapter-0697.md`, `chapter-0692.md` and `chapter-0693.md`, where the fourteen is tied with `chapter-0700.md`. TWELVE, the coda share was described as the lowest in the volume and Block 0002 is lower at 6.57 against this block's 6.99. THIRTEEN, the close record, `state/current.md` and `state/batch-summaries.md` all said Tovan Kest first appears in this volume at Chapter 0696, and he is named from `chapter-0654.md`, nineteen times on that one page; Chapter 0696 is where he refuses twice.

**AND ONE FIGURE WAS WRONG BY MORE THAN A MISCOUNT, AND IT IS THE ONE A SUCCESSOR MUST KNOW ABOUT. THE NAME BUDGET OF VOLUME 14 IS FOUR OF SIX AND NOT SIX OF SIX.** `Edric Hunn` is named in `chapters/volume-01/chapter-0026.md` as *Edric Hunn, marshal*, so he is an established return and not a new first name, he is not in `outline/volume-14.md` §13's cast table, and `outline/volume-14.md` §16 item 4 caps him separately at four printings in the volume. A batch phase entered him as a spend of this block's six-name budget, which made two budgets carry one name and made the volume read six of six. **The six-name budget, counted on first printings and by the convention at 1538, is `Salla Dunnock` at `chapter-0652.md`, `Halvard Rees` at `chapter-0660.md`, `Maud Trenchard` at `chapter-0663.md` and `Nell Drury` at `chapter-0697.md`: four spent, two slots unspent, and `Mara Vey`, `Hester Vane`, `Sera Quen` and `Tovan Kest` all returns.** The *five are spent and one remains* that stood at `state/open-threads.md` 562, 568, 587 and 606 was never supported by the names those items list, was carried forward unexamined through three blocks, and is now marked superseded in all four. **ONE ARITHMETIC IS NAMED AND NOT SETTLED: `outline/volume-14.md` §13 lists `Mara Vey` among the volume's six and 1538 records her as a return, and under the outline's reading the volume is five of six. Both readings are inside the budget, nothing downstream turns on it, and no chapter was written to depend on it.**

**THE LESSON, AND IT IS THE FOURTH TIME IT HAS BEEN LEARNED IN THIS PROJECT: a certificate is the last place a defect survives, because every downstream reader reads the certificate and not the chapters.** This one was written by a phase that had just recounted everything it was certifying, and nine of its figures were still wrong. **The instrument that catches all thirteen is `grep` over the state ledgers for every figure the close record prints, checked one at a time against the files, and no instrument in this project does that and it should be the standing habit of the next close.** No volume was closed twice and no batch was rewritten, and the correct shape of this repair was a short list of figures in five files and not one word of prose on a page.

---

## Volume 15, Batch 0001, the first block of Volume 15, Chapters 701 to 710

1618. **THE BLOCK'S FRAME, DERIVED AND NOT COPIED.** Day 930, the twenty-fifth of Mudmonth YR 318, a Sunday, is the only anchor, on the carried thirty-day month, and the chain is built forward from day 2310, the twenty-fifth of Goatmonth YR 321, a Monday, which is the last page of Volume 14 and is stated four times in that volume's close record. **Block 0001 is days 2311 to 2370 inclusive, sixty days, and the year turns inside it.** Goatmonth the first is day 2286 and Goatmonth the thirtieth is day 2315, so **YR 322 begins at day 2316, which is the first of Thawmonth and a Sunday, and Mudmonth the first is day 2346.** The ten days as written are **2311, 2313, 2316, 2320, 2324, 2327, 2334, 2341, 2346 and 2370**, and the printed datelines are **Goatmonth 26 and Goatmonth 28 YR 321, and Thawmonth 1, 5, 9, 12, 19 and 26, Mudmonth 1 and Mudmonth 25 YR 322.** **Five are market mornings, at 2313, 2320, 2327, 2334 and 2341, and five are not.** Two are the first of a month, and 2316 is the first of a month and the first day of a new year. **The printed weekday table at `outline/volume-13.md` §17 was not read for any day of this block and is wrong in five rows, of which day 2256 is the fifth.**

1619. **ONE INHERITED DATE IS CORRECTED IN THIS LEDGER AND IN NO CHAPTER, AND IT IS CORRECTED HERE BECAUSE A SUCCESSOR WILL FIND BOTH FIGURES.** Three ledgers stated that YR 322 does not begin until day 2341. **It begins at day 2316.** The derivation is two figures and the carried thirty-day month: Goatmonth is the twelfth month, Goatmonth the first is day 2286, Goatmonth the thirtieth is day 2315, and the next day is the first of a new year. **No chapter of Volume 14 is wrong, because Volume 14 ends on day 2310 and every one of its two hundred and seventy days is YR 321, and no chapter of Volume 15 was written to accommodate anything: Chapter 0703 is the first page in the manuscript dated in YR 322 and it says the date on the page.** `outline/volume-15.md` §16 item 8 carries this.

1620. **THE PROTAGONIST'S AGE IS RESOLVED ON THE PAGE IN CHAPTER 0708, IN THE DIRECTION THE VOLUME 14 CLOSE NAMED, AND THIRTY-TWO STANDS.** `outline/volumes/volume-14-close.md` §9 carried thirty-two to the end of Volume 14 and set the reconciliation at the start of Volume 15 where a character could notice it. **Chapter 0708, Thawmonth the twenty-sixth YR 322: the copyist of about twenty-six comes down the thirteen stairs once with a leaf and reads out the fen county road office roll of hands, dated YR 312, which says that Aren Kest was entered at the age of twenty-nine years, having served seven years before he was entered, signed at the entering by a master called Iowan Petch. Twenty-nine in YR 312 is thirty-eight in YR 322, and he is thirty-two.** Four people in that market street did the sum in about nine seconds and about five did not. **Nobody writes it down and nobody in nine counties finds out who asked the fen county road office for the page, and the asking is not traced in this volume and is not a new enemy.** He says the true thing out loud in the open and the true thing is that a paper which makes a man older makes him more use to somebody, and a man of thirty-eight with a master's entry is a man somebody can put at a gate and a man of thirty-two is not. **Nobody thanked him, nobody was angry with him, nobody defended him, and nine people wanted to say the figure was wrong and four had the standing to and did not.** The resolution is therefore: **the paper is wrong and the direction of the wrongness is named, and thirty-two stands on the page in all ten files of this block and in the rest of Volume 15.** A successor may not re-open it and may not edit Volume 14.

1621. **THE BELL ACROSS THE WATER GETS AN ORDINARY CAUSE AND IS NOT EXPLAINED, IN CHAPTER 0701, AND THIS IS A FACT AND NOT A RESOLUTION.** The last page of Volume 14 ended with a bell answering across a channel he had not rung. **Chapter 0701, Goatmonth the twenty-sixth YR 321: the top of a tide in Goatmonth is nine hundred yards wide over a nine hundred pace causeway, the man who keeps that gate rings the hand bell because the water is over it, and he rings it nine times.** About four people in nine counties say the man has rung it about nine hundred times in about eleven years and about five say nobody has asked him and he would not know. **Nobody in nine counties is ever told that the other bell exists, and no chapter of Volume 15 explains it, and a successor may not.**

1622. **THE GEOGRAPHY OF THE CAUSEWAY, THE FAR BANK, THE HEAD OF THE MADE ROAD AND THE SHOULDER, IN FULL, BECAUSE VOLUME 15 RUNS ON IT AND NO CHAPTER MAY CONTRADICT IT.** **The causeway stands at four days from the market town, where the made road comes off the shoulder of a hill onto a level stretch with the water on the low side.** It is nine hundred paces long and about nine feet wide at the crown with a low parapet on the seaward side, and a hand lamp is put on the coping at the low side every Monday afternoon in the winter, and the hand bell on the gate post is rung when the water is over. **Crossing it takes a man with a bad leg about nine minutes each way and the ebb at that hour takes about an hour and a half.** **On the far bank there is a made road going north with about nine hundred yards of laid kerb on both sides and then grass, and a shut hut about four hundred yards past the end of the kerb belonging to a man who keeps eels in weirs about two days north of it.** **The coast of returning roads is beyond the far bank and is not visited in this block.** **At the head of the made road, half a mile above the causeway, are a five-bar gate with the bar going in on the lane side, a drove, a byre, and about nine hundred yards of stone wall**, and about half a mile further on the low side is the walled yard of about nine hives belonging to a young man of about nineteen. **The lane leaves the made road at the head, is about nine feet wide, is not a made road and never has been, is about two miles long, turns twice and goes up over the shoulder of the hill at about a rise in nine.** A drove of about nine beasts goes up it about nine hundred yards every Thursday through a gap at the low end of the wall and stands in a field with a stone wall round it. **Past the shoulder there are about nine hundred yards of kerbed road and then about nine hundred yards of a line nine feet wide in the grass and nine stones set across the end of it at the same height, and a broom about nine acres on the east rise that comes on about the fourth of Rainmonth and is the only broom within a day's walk.** A successor may not move any of these distances and may not put the beekeeper's yard back eleven miles from the market town, which is where the first pass of Chapter 0705 had it and which was wrong and is repaired.
