and days 2755 to 2826, and the review phase is the phase that writes it, which is the house pattern and is visible in the history of `workspace/volume-15/batch-0004/PROMPT.md`.** **Two, that the first paragraph the next writer reads said a drove did not go up on either Thursday in the nine days of water**, and there was one Thursday in the nine days and a drove did go up it at about the sixth hour, and the same paragraph also said the practice had been eight for eighty-eight mornings, which is a calendar count at the twenty-seventh of Mudmonth and not a count of eights. **Both are corrected in `state/current.md` and neither was wrong on any page.** **Two were major.** **Three, that all ten closing paragraphs narrate the measuring instrument and that `state/current.md` forbade that in one sentence while permitting it in the next**, and the review was right that the two could not both stand. **THE DECISION IS THAT THE RULE IS RESTATED WITH ITS ONE EXCEPTION AND THE PAGES ARE NOT TOUCHED, and the reason is that `workspace/volume-16/batch-0002/PROMPT.md` §7 rule four requires the closing paragraph to name prices, a total, an over-figure, the mark conversion, the band, the usable values, the route below sixty and the position of its totals against every file before them, and that `outline/volume-16.md` §7 calls the column a column, and that the column is the last beat of every chapter in fifteen volumes. THE TENSION WITH THE GENERAL RULE IN `AGENTS.md` AGAINST A PAGE THAT IS A LIST OF STATISTICS IS REAL AND IS RECORDED IN `state/current.md` RATHER THAN DISMISSED, and what the review found is a contradiction in the state file and not a defect in the prose, and the finding has been fixed where it was true.** **Four, that the practice-morning derivation in `state/current.md` item one called the six lost mornings six of the seven NON-MARKET mornings of the nine days of water**, and a practice morning is a shut morning and not a non-market morning, and the sentence also dropped the clause that makes the arithmetic work, being that the seventh of the seven is the twenty-seventh and it ran. **The chapter at `chapter-0767.md` had it right and the state file had it wrong, and the state file now foots: seven of the nine days of water are practice mornings, the third and the fifth of the nine are market mornings, six went by with nobody on the floor, the seventh is the twenty-seventh, and the calendar count at that dateline is eighty-eight.**

1727. **THE THREE CALENDAR ERRORS THE SAME REVIEW PASS FOUND IN `state/current.md` ITSELF, NONE OF WHICH THE REVIEW REPORTED AND ALL OF WHICH ARE THE SAME DEFECT, BEING A FIGURE THAT WAS NOT DERIVED FROM THE DAY NUMBER.** **The frame it gave for this block's successor said the block's first day was a Wednesday and that its last day was a Friday and was not a market morning. THE FIRST DAY, 2755, IS A FRIDAY AND THE LAST DAY, 2826, IS A SATURDAY AND IS A MARKET MORNING, and the block carries two month boundaries and not one, being Longlight the first at 2766 which is a Tuesday and Harvestmonth the first at 2826 which is the block's own last day, and the earlier batch prompt's claim that 2746 was Longlight the first was twenty days out.** **The same paragraph also said the year turns again inside Volume 16 at 2766, and 2766 is Longlight the first and not a year, and the year turns once and once only inside this volume, at day 2676, and next at day 3036 which is thirty-four days past the end of the volume, and `outline/volume-16.md` §16 item eight says the year turns twice and names one day and is wrong in the same way.** **And the volume index's per-block day range was right and only the prose round it was wrong, which is the signature of a figure that was copied instead of derived.** **THE CHECKS THAT CATCH IT ARE THREE AND THEY COST NOTHING: derive any weekday as an offset from day 2310, which is a Monday and means offset zero, so offset one is a Tuesday and not a Monday; check a block's first day against the last day of the block before it, which here is 2754, a Thursday and a market morning; and check a month boundary against the carried month lengths, which are Mudmonth at sixty and every other month at thirty.** **AND TWO SMALLER THINGS THE SAME PASS FOUND WHILE DOING IT. One, four separate things in this county are about nine feet across, being the wall's gap, the width of the made road, the width of the standing broom and the distance of the man of thirty-two from the foot of the thirteen stairs, and Batch 0002 had printed the wall's gap as about five feet against the nine that all ten chapters of Batch 0001 print, so the earlier pass put the wall back and the review's point is a legibility one and no figure moved, because every one of the four is canon in an earlier volume. Two, `chapter-0769.md` printed Mara Vey twice, once in the paragraph that gives her the name and once at the door, and the second printing is now a pronoun and the name stands at one in the block and the file is the same length to the word.** **AND ONE LAPSE THE REVIEW DID NOT RAISE AND THIS PASS FOUND: Batch 0002's dateline of the first of Mudmonth is the first of a month and no page in that block advanced the count of firsts of a month that the hearing does not sit through, and the last figure on a page is the fifteenth at Thawmonth the first of YR 323 in `chapter-0760.md`, so Longlight the first inside Batch 0003 is the seventeenth and Harvestmonth the first is the eighteenth. THE COUNT IS NOT BACKFILLED INTO THE TEN PAGES AND IT IS NOT TO BE, and the gap is recorded here so that a successor carries the count forward from the fifteenth and does not begin it again.**

1728. **THE FRAME OF VOLUME 16 BATCH 0003, ITS TEN DATELINES, ITS TWO MONTH BOUNDARIES AND ITS SEVENTY-TWO DAYS, DERIVED FROM THE DAY NUMBER AND NOT FROM A ROW.** The block runs days 2755 to 2826 inclusive, which is seventy-two days, and it carries two month boundaries and not one, being Longlight the first at day 2766 and Harvestmonth the first at day 2826, **and the second of them IS THE BLOCK'S OWN LAST DAY.** **The ten datelines are 2755, 2760, 2766, 2772, 2779, 2783, 2791, 2800, 2811 and 2826, being Mudmonth the fiftieth and fifty-fifth, Longlight the first, the seventh, the fourteenth, the eighteenth and the twenty-sixth, Rainmonth the fifth and the sixteenth, and Harvestmonth the first, all of YR 323.** Their weekdays are Friday, Wednesday, Tuesday, Monday, Monday, Friday, Saturday, Monday, Friday and Saturday, and five of the ten are market mornings and five are not, **the five market mornings being 2791 and 2826, which are the only two Saturdays in the ten, and 2766, 2779 and 2800.** **THE BLOCK OPENS THE WAY ROUND FROM BATCH 0002 AND THE OPPOSITE WAY ROUND FROM THE BLOCK PROMPT'S OWN WARNING: day 2755 is a Friday and is not a market morning and the room is shut and the practice runs, and day 2826 is a Saturday and is a market morning and the room is open and the practice does not run.** Mudmonth runs 2706 to 2765 and is sixty days long, so days 2755 to 2765 are the last eleven days of it. Longlight runs 2766 to 2795, Rainmonth 2796 to 2825 and Harvestmonth 2826 to 2855. **THE YEAR DOES NOT TURN IN THIS BLOCK AND NO FIRST OF A YEAR FALLS IN IT, and every one of the ten datelines is YR 323 in the manuscript's own convention, which is the convention of the thirty files of Volume 16 already on disk.** **The market mornings inside the seventy-two days are twenty-one of them, being eleven Saturdays and ten Thursdays, and fifty-one mornings on which the room over the weigh-house is shut behind its own door, and the ten datelines carry five of each.**

1729. **THE SERIES ROW'S MIDPOINT REVERSAL IS MET IN THIS BLOCK, IT IS NOT A RELIEF, AND IT IS LEARNED OFF A ROAD AND OFF A PERSON.** The series row gives it in one line: the path that closes is not destroyed, and its silence becomes a political loss that Aren cannot solve with power. **What is on the page is that a man of thirty-two with a stick and a bad leg went up about eleven miles of made road and about nine hundred yards of cart track to the head of a cart track that has had nine hurdles across it since Batch 0001, and went up it twice in nine days, and at about the ninth hour of the seventh of Longlight one of the nine houses four hundred yards back put a light in one window.** **THE ROUTE IS NOT OPENED, THE NINE HURDLES ARE NOT LIFTED, NOTHING IS CUT, AND THE SILENCE IS NOT ENDED.** `outline/volume-16.md` section twelve's third condition for this block is a transfer witnessed and not conducted, and that is what the block is. **The reversal arrives as a political loss and not as a comfort, because the people at the end of the shut road do not want it opened: they set the hurdles themselves, and their name is the only thing keeping that road out of a roll, and a shut road with no name against it is a road the Marshal's hand in that notice cannot use.** **He learns the mechanism of nothing, he does not learn what a roadmark is, he does not learn why a piece of paper can shut a road, the word that names the founding paper is printed nowhere in the block, the separate leaf of the notebook that says *a person* is not looked at, and no character noticed the warmth of the left palm.**

1730. **THE FINDING COMES OFF A PERSON WHO HAS HAD IT AND HAS BEEN CAREFUL WITH IT, AND NOT OFF THE ROAD, AND THE KNOWLEDGE RULE AT `outline/volume-16.md` SECTION FOURTEEN IS SATISFIED IN BOTH DIRECTIONS.** **At `chapter-0771.md`, before he has learned anything, the man of about fifty-four says the more careful thing in a doorway in thirty-eight words and is asked a question nobody in nine counties has ever asked him, which is what the floor says when nobody is at it, and he answers that it says it and that a figure nobody has written down is a figure that dies with the man who said it and that stone is not a man.** The finding itself comes at `chapter-0774.md` off a voice behind the fourth door at Wethergill, in twenty-nine words, and it is not what he expected and it is not a comfort. **And he cannot lift it: the ninth thing is alive, unnamed, unheld and shut, and the chapter says on the page that a road nobody can name is a road nobody can shut either, and that a hundred and twenty-five days of shut with nine hurdles on it and a name against nothing is not a win and is not a loss and is a position.** **HE DOES NOT LEARN THE MECHANISM AND THE BLOCK NEVER NAMES WHAT A ROADMARK IS.** Nobody has been past the ninth of the nine stones and the far side of them is not shown and the dead grass in the scar stops at the ninth of them.

1731. **THE PRACTICE IS WRITTEN DOWN FOR THE FIRST TIME IN THE VOLUME'S HISTORY, IN A HAND THAT IS NOT HIS, AND THE HAND IS A WOMAN'S, AND IT IS NOT IN ANY DOCUMENT THIS MANUSCRIPT KEEPS.** `outline/volume-16.md` section seven says nothing about the practice is written down anywhere in Blocks 0001 to 0003 and section eight says this block writes it down, and the escalation sequence is the specific of the two and governs. **At `chapter-0778.md` the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps the room over the weigh-house writes nine figures and a date down in her own hand on the back leaf of the day-book, which carries no line and no ruling and is not the record, and the day-book stands at eleven entries on all ten datelines of the block, the register gains no entry on any of them, the fourth ruled head is empty, no fifth head is ruled, the copyist of about twenty-six ruled no head that day and has been told about none of it, and nothing about the practice and nothing about the household is in any document.** **THE REASON IS EARNED IN HER TRADE AND NOT ASKED FOR: he asks where a figure goes so that somebody reads it, in thirty-seven words, and the answer is that a roll wants a name against a line and he has no name and no line and a register wants a finding and there is no finding under any of the ten questions, and she then says in thirty words that she will write it where a keeper writes the thing that is not an entry and that he will not read it and will not tell her he has.** **It COSTS HER SOMETHING AND THE COST IS NAMED ON THE PAGE: a keeper of records has one thing that is hers and it is her hand, and about fourpence a year is what a person is paid for the side of a book the record is on.** **THE FOUR LINES OF CHALK ON THE STONE SILL AT `chapter-0770.md` ARE BUILT ON AND ARE NOT EXPLAINED, NOT READ OUT LOUD, NOT PRAISED AND NOT ENTERED ANYWHERE, and she takes the stick of chalk off that sill and puts it in the press drawer on the Monday after and the man of about fifty-four has not asked her for it.** **No clerk in nine counties will ever look at the leaf, and the page says so, and nothing in this block reads as a cure.**

1732. **THE NAMES, THE TWO SPENDS, AND THE RETURN, AND THE CAP.** A capitalised two-word sweep over the ten files returns exactly three strings: **`Wyn Fell` at 1**, about forty-four, who keeps the fourth house at Wethergill and speaks through a shut door at `chapter-0774.md` in twenty-nine words and gives the only name anybody at that place has given to anybody from this county in nine years; **`Abel Chinn` at 1**, about thirty-one, who keeps a stone trough at the bottom of that cart track and fills it twice a week and carries water up eleven miles on his own back, first printed at `chapter-0775.md`; and **`Mara Vey` at 1**, a return and not a spend, printed at `chapter-0778.md`. **THE VOLUME 16 NAME BUDGET IS SIX AND FOUR ARE NOW SPENT, being `Renn Ottery` and `Pell Arkwright` in Batch 0001 and these two here, AND TWO REMAIN FOR BLOCKS 0004 AND 0005 AND NOT TWO IN ONE CHAPTER.** The protagonist is thirty-two and was not recounted. `Edric Hunn` and `Hunn` are printed zero times across the thirty files of Volume 16 and every reference to the instrument is that notice or the Marshal's hand.

1733. **THE COST OF THE BLOCK IS ON THE PAGE AND IS NOT CURED IN IT, AND IT IS NOT A MAN LOSING AN ILLUSION ABOUT A RECORD FOR THE THIRD YEAR RUNNING.** The use of one ear on the side the stick is on, which takes rain, drip, market noise, six hundred people and seven people breathing in the dark, and which the block states as a half deafness he will have on that side when this is over. The use of one leg, which takes eleven miles of made road in four hours when it is walked before light and in about nine when it is not, two trips up a cart track, about three hours standing on a rise, a knee that goes down once on the seventh of Longlight and gets him up on the second attempt with nobody seeing it but him, and four hours on a stone floor at the weigh-house. **AND THE NEW ONE, WHICH IS NAMED TWICE AND IS NOT A VISION: that a silence he can now name is not a thing he can carry, that being the man who went and looked is a job and not an honour, and that a thing somebody can name is not a thing anybody holds.** **THE BLOCK'S OTHER COST IS THE FIGURE HE CANNOT GIVE, at `chapter-0779.md`, where the woman of about fifty-one who keeps the inn asks him for a figure in her own trade and he has not got one, and a board with nine blanks on it stays blank.**

1734. **THE ONE SYSTEM PANEL OF THE VOLUME IS SPENT HERE AND NOWHERE ELSE.** It is at `chapter-0776.md`, set alone as its own paragraph, not spoken aloud, carrying no number, and it is **seventeen words and not the fifteen this item printed before the repair pass at 1741**, being Holding is said by two and not heard by nine, and the nine are not the holding, counted on the panel as printed and with the bold markers off. Nobody else on that floor heard anything, he has never told anybody what it was, and nobody in nine counties has asked him. **It is not spent on the reversal, because the reversal is learned off a road and off a person, and it is spent on the fact that a practice is said by two and the rest are the room, and that the man who began it has been in the room for about four and a half years and has never been one of the two.** Blocks 0004 and 0005 print none. The Many-Handed is not crossed again.

1735. **THE ONE TIME SOMEBODY WAS ASKED TO ACCOUNT FOR A THING DONE ON THEIR OWN ACCOUNT IN THIS BLOCK, AND IT WAS DONE ONCE, IN A MAN'S TRADE, AND IT IS THE ONLY SUCH ASK IN THE BLOCK.** At `chapter-0772.md` the roadkeeper of about fifty-two had set a bar across a soft place in the track below the weigh-house on the Monday before last and the woman of about fifty-one had laid broom straw over it, both on their own account, and at `chapter-0770.md` the page said that nobody in this county has ever asked a person that and that this was the first morning anybody could have and nobody did. **At `chapter-0772.md` the man of thirty-two does ask him, standing out of the rut, in that man's trade, and the answer is nineteen words: carts were cutting it and he keeps four miles and nobody asked him to and nobody was going to, and the page says that it cost the man of about fifty-two more than the bar cost him, because a man who has given an account has a thing to keep.** **Nobody has yet asked the woman of about fifty-one about the broom straw, and nobody has asked either of the two mendings from the roadkeeper's side, and a successor may not turn either mending into a practice with a rule.**

1736. **THE MAN OF FORTY-ONE SAYS SIXTEEN WORDS IN THE WHOLE BLOCK AND IS ASKED NOTHING BEFORE OR AFTER, AT `chapter-0776.md`, IN HIS OWN TRADE: I hold the wall. The gap is nine feet and I am not starting the ninth.** His eleven words on a shoulder at `chapter-0767.md` remain all he said in Batch 0002 and are permanent. Neither refusal of Volume 15 was reopened, argued with, softened, or put to either of the two men. **The ninth yard of the wall has not been started, the man of thirty-two stands in the gap for about nine minutes and puts his hand on the near end of the eight finished yards and takes it off, and the line of stone on the bank two miles off with the mallet on top of it is not touched and nobody asked the man of forty-one about it.**

1737. **THE TWO THINGS `outline/volume-16.md` SECTION ELEVEN HANDS TO BLOCKS 0004 AND 0005 ARE SET UP HERE IN THIS BLOCK'S OWN TERMS, AND NEITHER IS CONDUCTED.** **The first, a refusal that is also a refusal to govern, is set up at `chapter-0780.md`: about nine people in nine trades said one thing each out loud on the first of Mudmonth and none of them touched the paper, and not one of those nine has since offered any other person in nine counties a place to put their name either, and the page says that a refusal which is also a refusal to govern is the shape of the thing this county is going to have to answer, and that a county which can only refuse is a county that cannot hold. THE NINE ARE NOT A BODY AND WERE NOT ASKED FOR AN ACCOUNT.** **The second, a sealed route that somebody has claimed can be made to speak, claimed by somebody and not by him, is set up at `chapter-0780.md` where the roadkeeper of about fifty-two says at the boards, in his own trade, to about nine people, in thirty words, that a bar will get up that track if a barrow can and that he has said so out loud and that he is not going to be the one who tries it, and the page says that a claim is not a hand on a hurdle and that a man who can make a road speak and will not is the most dangerous thing in this county, which is a man who knows how. NOTHING WAS TRIED AND NO HURDLE WAS TOUCHED.**

1738. **THE MONEY OF THIS BLOCK WITH THE COLLISION SET REBUILT, AND THE CLOSING CLAIM THAT IS TRUE.** **THE INSTRUMENT IS THE PRINTED TOTAL, taken as the spelled phrase in EVERY SPELLING THE MANUSCRIPT PRINTS ACROSS THE WHOLE HUNDRED BAND, being the two-hundred and three-hundred band as well as the one-hundred band, plus the digit form, run over every file whole and not sliced.** **REBUILT OVER ALL SEVEN HUNDRED AND SEVENTY FILES THAT STOOD BEFORE THIS BLOCK IT GIVES TWELVE FREE VALUES IN THE BAND SIXTY TO THREE HUNDRED AND FORTY-THREE, being 212, 218, 230, 254, 266, 273, 281, 308, 314, 321, 326 and 329, AND EVERY ONE OF THE TWELVE FAILS THE BARRED-OVER-FIGURE FILTER, their over-figures being 20, 26, 38, 14, 26, 33, 41, 20, 26, 33, 38 and 41, EVERY ONE OF THEM ONE OF THE BARRED SIX 14, 20, 26, 33, 38 AND 41. THE USABLE FREE SET IN THE BAND IS ZERO AND IT HAS BEEN ZERO SINCE BATCH 0002 CLOSED, AND THE ROUTE BELOW SIXTY IS STILL SHUT, being that ten distinct non-barred prices cannot sum below fifty-five, every total from fifty-five to fifty-nine is printed, and the only free value under sixty is fifty-two, which no ten distinct prices can reach.** **THE TEN TOTALS OF THIS BLOCK ARE 85, 113, 142, 163, 186, 208, 229, 250, 274 AND 297 PENCE, ten distinct and ascending, and all ten are printed already and the closing paragraph of all ten files says so on the page and gives the reason, which is arithmetic, and each of the ten says that the band from sixty to three hundred and forty-three has been searched whole again and gives twelve values that no file in the manuscript before them has printed and that every one of the twelve would give an over-figure that cannot be priced.** **Every column foots four ways with the forty-eight-pence conversion checked separately from the sum, the over-figures are 37, 17, 46, 19, 42, 16, 37, 10, 34 and 9, and no barred numeral is a price or an over-figure or a printed total in any of the ten.** **LIFTING THE CEILING IS AN OUTLINE DECISION AND IS NOT A WRITER'S. NONE OF THE WORKING VOCABULARY OF THE INSTRUMENT IS ON ANY PAGE, and the four permitted phrases stand exactly once in each of the ten closing paragraphs and not one word more.**

1739. **THE MEASUREMENTS OF THIS BLOCK, RECOUNTED ON THE FILES AND NOT INHERITED, AGAINST THE VOLUME 13 BASELINE, AND THE COLUMN BEFORE AND AFTER THE REPAIR PASS AT 1741, BOTH PRINTED.** The per-file word counts after that pass are **4,031 / 4,251 / 4,045 / 4,041 / 4,055 / 4,026 / 3,665 / 4,241 / 3,998 / 4,062**, the block total is **40,415**, the mean is **4,041.5 and 4,041 truncated**, the minimum is 3,665 at `chapter-0777.md` and the maximum is 4,251 at `chapter-0772.md`, and no file is below three thousand words, **and this item printed 3,940 / 4,054 / 4,039 / 4,035 / 4,010 / 4,017 / 3,654 / 4,229 / 3,992 / 4,045, a total of 40,015 and a mean of 4,001.5, before that pass and correctly for the files as they then stood.** `about` is **23.57 / 24.94 / 26.45 / 22.52 / 21.45 / 23.10 / 25.10 / 22.40 / 25.76 / 22.90** per 1,000, floor at `chapter-0775.md` and ceiling at `chapter-0773.md`, and **no page is at or above 27.00**, and it was 24.62 / 25.90 / 26.49 / 22.80 / 22.69 / 23.15 / 25.18 / 22.46 / 25.80 / 22.99 before the pass. **THE CENSUS ON THE INSTRUMENT NAMED AT `state/continuity.md` 1708 IS BASIS A 353 PARAGRAPHS AND ZERO OPENERS AND BASIS B 299 AND ZERO, both of which are unchanged by the repair pass because the pass added and removed no paragraph, and THE BASIS B DENOMINATOR IS RE-DERIVABLE FROM FIVE NAMED EXCLUSIONS AND NOT TWO: 353 LESS TEN DATELINES LESS TWENTY-THREE PARAGRAPHS THAT OPEN WITH A QUOTATION MARK LESS THE ONE SYSTEM PANEL, WHICH IS ITS OWN PARAGRAPH AT `chapter-0776.md` AND IS NEITHER A DATELINE NOR DIALOGUE, LESS THE TEN BOLD `**And the cost of**` COST CODAS, LESS THE TEN BOLD CLOSING MONEY COLUMNS, WHICH IS 299. The clause about the panel was the one missing at 1691 and the two the review of this block found missing at `logs/batch-0003.review.log` are the cost codas and the money columns, and a successor who wants a third denominator must say which of those five groups the third figure leaves out.** The thank family is **thirty-seven** uses and every one of the thirty-seven is a negation of thanking or a plain statement that a person was not thanked, **and this item printed thirty-six before the repair pass and was one short on the files, the count being 8 / 3 / 3 / 3 / 3 / 4 / 3 / 4 / 3 / 3 by file,** and **NOBODY IS THANKED ANYWHERE IN THIS BLOCK.** The build vocabulary is zero and interiority is present in all ten files. **THE DUPLICATE COUNT OVER ALL SEVEN HUNDRED AND EIGHTY FILES IS THIRTY-SIX GROUPS AND THIRTY-SEVEN SURPLUS OCCURRENCES, AND THIS BLOCK ADDS ZERO GROUPS AND ZERO SURPLUS OCCURRENCES, having begun the pass at seven groups and eighteen surplus occurrences and been cut back by varying five stock paragraphs on nine datelines, and the same instrument over the seven hundred and seventy prior files gives the same thirty-six and thirty-seven, which is the check that the zero is a zero.** **ALL TWENTY-THREE PRINTED SPEECHES FOOT OFF THE WORDS PRINTED BESIDE THEM AND THE FOUR SPEECHES THAT CARRY NO COUNT CARRY NO COUNT.** Zero em dashes, en dashes, semicolons, exclamation marks, single asterisks, straight double quotes and curly apostrophes, per-line bold parity even on every line, every file ends in a newline, and curly doubles balanced in every file.

1740. **WHAT DID NOT MOVE IN THIS BLOCK, AND IT IS THE WHOLE OF THE PROHIBITION LIST.** No second System panel is printed. The antagonist's name is printed zero times and every reference to the instrument is that notice or the Marshal's hand. Neither refusal of Volume 15 was reopened, argued with, softened, or put to either of the two men, and the man of forty-one was not asked a second question and says sixteen words in this block at `chapter-0776.md` and no more. Nobody is past the ninth of the nine stones and the far side of them is not shown and the dead grass stops at the ninth of them, and nobody is shown standing behind all nine hurdles either, and the omission is not an oversight. No route was opened, closed or cut and no hurdle was lifted and no stone was laid and the mallet was not picked up and the three courses of sill, the line of stone and the mallet are in none of the ten files. The mechanism is not explained and no chapter says what a roadmark is and the separate leaf of the notebook that says a person is not looked at and no character noticed the warmth of the left palm. No head was filled or ruled, no finding was put under any of the ten questions, the half leaf stays face down, the cloth is still over the bench at the low end of those boards and nobody asked what is under it, and the three series are three series and the band figure is not printed. The two written refusals did not become a body, a practice, a rule or a head, the nine households at the mill lane did not become a body, the nine houses at Wethergill did not become a body, and the nine people who refused the roll at the boards on the first of Mudmonth did not become a body and were not asked for an account. Nobody was sent to the city four hundred miles off, the shut road four hundred miles off stays shut, the road that went quiet in Thawmonth YR 319 is still quiet, the carter's mate of about twenty-two is not found, the coast's twenty-second notch is not cut, and nobody traced who asked the fen county road office for the roll. The four holes in the arrangement are four and none is called small and none is resolved and no fifth was opened. The scar is a hundred and seventy-four days old at the first dateline and two hundred and forty-six at the last and is not cured and is not going to close over, the standing broom is about nine hundred yards of a line nine feet wide and will not either, the gap in the wall is about nine feet long and about four feet high and the ninth yard has not been started, and the four things about nine feet across carry the noun with the number on every page that prints one. The brood stands at five frames under about nine straw skirts and no sixth has gone in. Nobody is thanked in any chapter and in any state file this block wrote.

## The repair pass on Volume 16 Batch 0003, run by a phase that did not write it, items 1741 to 1745, from `logs/batch-0003.review.log`

1741. **WHAT THE REVIEW FOUND, IN ONE PARAGRAPH, AND IT IS FOURTEEN FINDINGS AND NOT ONE OF THEM IS A MATTER OF TASTE.** The review read the ten files of this block against this block's own hand-off and reported three high, six medium and five low findings, **and the high three are one class: a figure was derived once and then reused.** Nine of the ten drove paragraphs date the Thursday before their own dateline and eight of those nine dates are wrong, **being that Mudmonth is sixty days long and three of the eight, the sixty-fifth, the seventy-second and the ninety-first, are dates that cannot exist at all.** The drove *counts* on all ten were right and the dateline weekdays were right, so the error is in one attached clause and not in the chain. `chapter-0772.md` then had three interlocking errors of its own, being a stated walk of about nine hours over a body that walks it in four, a roadkeeper placed a mile on the wrong side of the man he is talking to, and the same hour of the same evening printed eleven miles apart. And the chalk on the stone sill was drowned by rain in two chapters of this block although `chapter-0770.md` had put it under a hood where the rain does not reach, which is the block's founding image. **THE FINDING A SUCCESSOR HAS TO CARRY IS THAT A CALENDAR DERIVED ONCE AND THEN HANDED ON IS A COPY, AND THAT A COPY OF A CHAIN LOOKS EXACTLY LIKE A CHAIN UNTIL SOMEBODY ASKS FOR A DAY OF A SIXTY-DAY MONTH THAT IS NOT THERE.**

1742. **THE FOURTEEN FINDINGS, WHAT WAS DONE ABOUT EACH, AND THE TWO THINGS THAT WERE FIXED WITHOUT BEING ASKED FOR.** **One,** the eight wrong drove dates, all fixed, and `chapter-0771.md` and `chapter-0772.md` were already right at the forty-ninth of Mudmonth because that Thursday had not been superseded. **Two,** `chapter-0772.md`'s spine: the dateline now prints the walk as between the fifth hour and the ninth, the roadkeeper's soft place is a mile short of the trough at the bottom of the cart track and not a mile short of the weigh-house, the four miles he keeps under a written paper are named as the four miles *below* the weigh-house, and the return is the Thursday morning instead of the ninth hour of the same Wednesday, which he could not have done and had slept at the trough. **Three,** the chalk: the four lines are now dry and sharp under the hood at `chapter-0771.md` with one pale thumb-drag across the end of the top line that he reads for a moment as the first stroke of a letter, and the cost codas at `chapter-0771.md` and `chapter-0780.md` say four lines under a sill the rain does not reach. **Four,** the System panel is **seventeen** words and three state files said fifteen, corrected at `state/current.md`, at 1734 above and at `state/chapter-summaries.md`. **Five,** the back leaf of the day-book is **nine figures and a date** and not nine on one page and a figure on another, corrected at `chapter-0780.md`, at `state/current.md` and at 1731 above. **Six,** `chapter-0777.md` no longer reports at line 15 that four hundred people had already been told the thing on the morning the chapter does not reach until line 35. **Seven,** `chapter-0771.md`'s dateline said a man of thirty-four with a stick and he is thirty-two, and a man of about thirty-four with a satchel is a different person who appears at `chapter-0776.md`. **Eight,** `chapter-0780.md`'s garbled nine feet off at the wall is now nine feet off her at the wall. **Nine,** the Monday promised in a wet yard at `chapter-0771.md` is now the Wednesday, and the Wednesday is the day he walks, so the promise is kept on the page. **Ten,** `state/phase-ledger.json` is controller-owned and was not touched and a successor may not touch it. **Eleven,** `workspace/volume-16/batch-0004/PROMPT.md` did not exist and now does, because `AGENTS.md` requires the finishing phase to write exactly one next-batch prompt and the workflow's fallback is a one-paragraph generic continuation prompt, which is not this project's practice. **Twelve,** the block prompt's own five-and-five on market mornings is wrong and the ten datelines give two, corrected at `state/current.md` and `state/chapter-summaries.md` and recorded for the successor at `state/open-threads.md` 720. **Thirteen,** Basis B was under-specified and all five of its exclusions are now named at `state/current.md` and at 1739 above. **Fourteen,** one frame carried two hundred and thirty-five sentences and a hundred and eighteen of them were re-voiced, so that the batch now reads have been saying that forty, have said to each other that thirty-nine, have kept saying that thirty-nine and have said since that one hundred and seventeen, **and the ten chapters no longer all answer the printed request of the fifteenth of Frostmonth in the same eleven words.** **THE TWO FIXED WITHOUT BEING ASKED FOR, both of the same class as a finding above:** `chapter-0775.md` had the man of thirty-two walking down eleven miles from a cart track head he had promised at `chapter-0774.md` not to go up a third time, and arriving at a trough an hour before it and a market town three hours after it, and he now goes up to the trough on the Saturday before and lies two nights under the hedge beside it and comes down on the Monday, which is the same chapter, the same trough, the same man at the trough and the same speech of twenty-six words. And `chapter-0774.md` printed nine days between two trips that are six days apart, in the dateline, in the chorus and in the body's own sentence about what he got wrong.

1743. **THE FOUR FIGURES THAT MOVED, AND THE THREE THAT DID NOT, AND EVERY COUNT RE-RUN AFTER THE LAST PROSE EDIT.** The word counts moved, and the block total is 40,415 and not 40,015, and the `about` band moved, and the thank family is thirty-seven and not thirty-six, and the census did not move at all, being Basis A 353 and Basis B 299 and zero openers on both, because the repair added and removed no paragraph in any of the ten files. **The duplicate-paragraph count over all seven hundred and eighty files at twelve words or more is thirty-six groups and thirty-seven surplus occurrences and this block still contributes zero of both after the pass, and the same instrument over the seven hundred and seventy files that stand before it gives the same thirty-six and thirty-seven, which is the check that the zero is a zero.** The money is untouched: all ten totals, eighty-five, one hundred and thirteen, one hundred and forty-two, one hundred and sixty-three, one hundred and eighty-six, two hundred and eight, two hundred and twenty-nine, two hundred and fifty, two hundred and seventy-four and two hundred and ninety-seven pence, still foot, still all already printed, still disclosed on the page with the reason, and no barred numeral is a price or an over-figure or a printed total in any of the ten. **All twenty-three printed speeches still foot off the words printed beside them, and the seventeen-word panel is now counted correctly, and the eleven-word drag that replaced the wet lines at `chapter-0771.md` is a sentence and not a counted speech and carries no count, which is the rule the block already used for the four speeches that carry none.**

1744. **WHAT THIS PASS DID NOT DO, AND THE PROOF THAT IT DID NOT DO IT.** No plot beat was moved, no chapter was rewritten, no name was spent and no name was unspent, no route was opened and no hurdle was lifted and no route was shut, no System panel was added and the one panel stands where it stood, `Edric Hunn` is printed zero times across the thirty files, neither refusal of Volume 15 was reopened and the man of forty-one was not asked a second question, nobody went past the ninth of the nine stones, the ninth yard of the wall was not started and the mallet was not picked up, the half leaf is still face down, the cloth is still over the bench, and **the last dateline is still the eighteenth first of a month in a row with nothing at the boards on any of them.** **Nobody is thanked anywhere, and the thirty-seven uses of the thank family are all negations, and this pass added no sentence of thanks to any chapter and none to any state file.** The planned ending is untouched and no final enemy was introduced and the antagonist remains Edric Hunn at zero printings with two left for Blocks 0004 and 0005, both documents.

1745. **WHAT A SUCCESSOR INHERITS FROM THIS PASS, WHICH IS SIX THINGS AND NONE OF THEM IS A FIGURE.** **One,** the drove paragraph's calendar date, which is a derived value and must be derived on the day of the week and not carried from the last chapter, and the rule that catches it is that Mudmonth is sixty days and every other month is thirty, so a day of month above thirty in Mudmonth is impossible. **Two,** that the founding image of this volume is four lines of chalk **under a hood where the rain does not reach**, and any successor page that puts water on those four lines is wrong. **Three,** that the leaf at the back of the day-book carries nine figures and a date and the names of nobody, and that the chalk is in a press drawer and nobody has asked the man of about fifty-four for it. **Four,** that the soft place the roadkeeper barred is a mile short of the weigh-house and is **not** on the four miles he keeps, and that the four miles he keeps are the four miles below the weigh-house, and that the answer of nineteen words is unchanged and still foots. **Five,** that the walk north from the weigh-house to the trough takes about four hours before light and about nine otherwise, and that he turns back at the gate in failing light, sleeps on the dry side of the trough and walks down on the next morning. **Six,** that `workspace/volume-16/batch-0004/PROMPT.md` is on disk and was written by the phase that repaired this block, and that a successor may not write a second prompt for Batch 0004 and may not rebuild it, **and the clause forbidding a volume-close prompt for Volume 16 is withdrawn by the review repair pass at 1759 onward, because Batch 0005 is the fifth of five blocks and its own prompt section nine instructs it to create exactly one `workspace/volume-16/close/PROMPT.md`, and `AGENTS.md` and `PHASE_SYSTEM.md` both put a close prompt where a close prompt goes.**

## Volume 16, Batch 0004, the fourth block of Volume 16, Chapters 781 to 790, "The Roll", items 1746 to 1758, and the highest number in this file is now 1758

1746. **THE FRAME OF THIS BLOCK, ITS TEN DATELINES, ITS THREE MONTH BOUNDARIES AND ITS SEVENTY-TWO DAYS, DERIVED FROM THE DAY NUMBER AND NOT FROM A ROW.** The block runs days 2827 to 2898 inclusive, which is seventy-two days, and it carries three month boundaries, being Fallowmonth the first at day 2856, Embermonth the first at day 2886, and Wolfmonth the first at day 2896, **and the third of those is inside the block and no chapter is dated on it and no chapter prints a date later than the thirteenth of Embermonth, which is day 2898, because the fourteenth is day 2899 and that is the first day of Batch 0005.** **THE TEN DATELINES AS PRINTED ARE 2827, 2833, 2835, 2852, 2856, 2868, 2879, 2886, 2892 and 2898, being Harvestmonth the second, the eighth, the tenth and the twenty-seventh, Fallowmonth the first, the thirteenth and the twenty-fourth, and Embermonth the first, the seventh and the thirteenth, all YR 323.** Their weekdays are Sunday, Saturday, Monday, Thursday, Monday, Saturday, Wednesday, Wednesday, Tuesday and Monday. **ONE DATELINE WAS MOVED AND ONE ONLY, being 2843, the eighteenth of Harvestmonth and a Tuesday, which was moved to 2835, the tenth of Harvestmonth and a Monday, so that the three answers to the roll could be given out loud inside one week, on Thursday 2831, Saturday 2833 and Monday 2835, being a Thursday market morning, a Saturday market morning and a non-market morning with about nine people in it.** **THE THREE OF THE BLOCK'S THREE MARKET MORNINGS ON THE TEN DATELINES ARE 2833, 2852 AND 2868 AND THE SEVEN THAT ARE NOT CARRY THE ROOM OVER THE WEIGH-HOUSE SHUT BEHIND ITS OWN DOOR, and the block as written opens the way round Batch 0002 had it and not the way round Batch 0003 had it: day 2827 is a Sunday with the room shut and the practice running and the last day of the block, day 2898, is a Monday with the room shut and the practice running.** The market mornings inside the seventy-two days are twenty, being ten Saturdays and ten Thursdays, and fifty-two mornings on which the room is shut behind its own door, and the ten datelines carry three of each and a successor is to derive that count and not to inherit it.

1747. **THE ROLL IS A DOCUMENT AND IT IS THE CULL'S MACHINERY, AND NOBODY IN NINE COUNTIES FILLED IT IN, TORE IT UP, TOOK IT DOWN OR PUT A NAME AGAINST ANY ROAD.** It arrives up the made road in the ordinary post at `chapter-0781.md`, day 2827, on a Sunday, at about the eleventh hour, in a Crown's cart that does not know what it is carrying, and it is one printed sheet with a ruled line for each of nine counties, **the fourth of the nine lines being this county's**, and one space on each line for one name, and it says in print that the name required is the name of the person who keeps the roads of that county and that a road with no name against it on the twenty-third of Fallowmonth is to be shut, and that Tuesday is fifty-one days off at that dateline. **It was on the boards seventy-one days at the block's last dateline and its nine lines were empty on it and on a second copy, and no gate was shut and no bar was put on and no hurdle was lifted and nothing was filled in and nothing was torn up.** About four people in nine counties had said on the first of Mudmonth that a roll had four lines on it and there are nine, and the page says so at `chapter-0781.md`. A Crown's cart brought a second copy up the made road in the ordinary post at `chapter-0788.md`, day 2886, the twentieth first of a month, and nailed it over the first without taking the first one down, and inside the covering paper folded over the back of it there was a sheet folded in four with one sentence on it in a hand that is not the hand of any clerk this county has ever had, **and that sentence says that nobody in this county is paid to keep a road, and that a form a county cannot fill is not to be filled by writing somebody else's name on it, and that a county which will not govern ought to say so in writing to the people it is not governing, and NOBODY IN NINE COUNTIES HAS SAID WHO WROTE IT AND NOBODY IS GOING TO BE ASKED.** The protagonist read it, put it back the way it was, asked nobody and was asked nothing.

1748. **THE ANTAGONIST'S NAME IS PRINTED FOR THE FIRST TIME IN THIS VOLUME, ONCE, IN A DOCUMENT, AT THE FOOT OF A COVERING PAPER, UNDER THE MARSHAL'S HAND, AND NEVER IN A MOUTH.** It is at `chapter-0781.md`, on the covering paper folded over the back of the roll, written out in full as **Edric Hunn**, and the man of thirty-two looked at it for about as long as it takes to turn a cart twice over and did not ask about it, and nobody in that street asked him anything. **THE PRINTING IS ONE ACROSS ALL FORTY FILES OF VOLUME 16 AND ONE OF THE VOLUME'S CAP OF TWO, and the second and last printing is reserved for Batch 0005 and is a document, and Blocks 0001, 0002 and 0003 printed it zero times and this block printed it once.** Every other reference to the instrument across the ten files is that notice, the Marshal's hand, the roll, the second copy, or the folded sheet, and `Hunn` on its own is not a printing and the bare surname stands nowhere on any of the ten pages.

1749. **THE THREE INCOMPATIBLE ANSWERS, WHO GAVE THEM, ON WHICH DAYS, AND WHY NO COUNTY CAN HOLD TWO OF THEM.** **One, at the boards in that market street on Thursday the sixth of Harvestmonth, day 2831, a market morning, reported with its words printed at `chapter-0782.md`, given by Brida Lenth, about forty-six, who keeps a lime kiln four miles north of that weigh-house and a stone-walled yard behind it, with lime dust to the elbow, in thirty-one words: she has burnt lime for this county nine years and carried the stone for it on her own arms and she is not going on a line under somebody else's hand. The ground is that a keeper's name is a thing that is hers and is not to be entered under another hand.** **Two, at the same boards on Saturday the eighth of Harvestmonth, day 2833, a market morning with about four hundred people in the street, live, given by Pell Arkwright, about twenty-seven, who drives lime out of Wethergill and has not been paid for about two hundred and thirty loads, in forty-two words: a road with no name against it is a road nobody can shut, and a shut road at Wethergill has been shut nine years on nobody's paper, and he is not going to be the one who puts a name on yours.** **Three, in the lane at the foot of those thirteen stairs on Monday the tenth of Harvestmonth, day 2835, not a market morning, with about nine people in the lane, live, given by the woman of about twenty-nine who keeps the room over that weigh-house, in forty-five words: a form nobody is paid to fill is a form a county cannot fill, and there is nobody in nine counties paid to govern, and about fourpence a year is what a person is paid for the side of a book the record is on.** **THE THREE ARE INCOMPATIBLE IN THE SENSE THAT NO COUNTY CAN HOLD TWO OF THEM AT ONCE, AND NOT IN THE SENSE THAT ONE OF THEM IS CORRECT: the first refuses every name on the ground that a name is the keeper's own and is not to be entered under another hand, the second refuses on the ground that a road with no name against it is a road nobody can shut, and the third insists that a county which will not fill a form is a county which will not govern and which ought to be paid to govern.** The third answer is the shape Batch 0003 set up at `chapter-0780.md` coming due, and the folded sheet at `chapter-0788.md` is that same refusal in writing. **NONE OF THE THREE IS THE PROTAGONIST'S AND HE SAYS NOTHING AT ALL, AND THE PAGE SAYS HE SAYS NOTHING AT ALL, at `chapter-0782.md`, at `chapter-0783.md`, at `chapter-0784.md` where about nine people ask him four questions and he answers none of them, and at `chapter-0790.md`.**

1750. **THE NAMES, THE ONE SPEND, AND THE CAP.** `Brida Lenth` is printed once, at `chapter-0782.md`, and is a new first printing and **THE ONE NAME THIS BLOCK SPENT AGAINST THE VOLUME'S BUDGET OF SIX**, being the fifth of the six, being `Renn Ottery` and `Pell Arkwright` in Batch 0001 and `Wyn Fell` and `Abel Chinn` in Batch 0003 and this one here. **`Pell Arkwright` is printed once and is a return and costs nothing, and `Edric Hunn` is printed once and is debited to his own cap and not to the name budget.** A capitalised two-word sweep over the ten files returns exactly three strings, being `Edric Hunn`, `Brida Lenth` and `Pell Arkwright`, and one sentence opener. **ONE SLOT REMAINS FOR BATCH 0005 AND IT IS THE LAST OF THE VOLUME'S SIX.** The protagonist is thirty-two and was not recounted.

1751. **THE RELATIONSHIP COMES DUE IN PUBLIC AND IS NOT DECLARED, AND IT IS DONE IN FOUR SEPARATE WAYS ACROSS FOUR CHAPTERS, AND NONE OF THEM IS A STATEMENT.** **One, at `chapter-0782.md`, the copyist of about twenty-six comes down those thirteen stairs with a sheet in her hand and puts it into the hands of the woman of about twenty-nine and not into those of the man of thirty-two, because she knows where it goes, and does not stop at either of them and says nothing about it, and about four people in that lane worked out inside nine seconds that a copyist who knows where a sheet goes hands it to one person and not to another, and nobody in nine counties commented on it at all.** **Two, at `chapter-0783.md`, the man of about fifty-four who keeps that weigh-house floor says sixteen words to the man of thirty-two in that lane about the fourth line of the roll, and the sixteen words use the word keeper about the woman of about twenty-nine, and nobody said anything back to either of them and nobody in nine counties has said since that the word was wrong or that it was right.** **Three, at `chapter-0785.md`, the second lamp at the foot of those thirteen stairs went out at about the sixth hour and stood there lit until about the eighth, and about four people in that lane had worked out inside nine seconds that a lamp at the foot of a stair is not a thing a keeper leaves burning for want of oil.** **Four, the two of them are not described together on any of the ten pages and no chapter ends on them, and nothing about the two of them is in any document, and the legal form stays deferred to Volume 18, and nobody comments on the four and a half years and nobody is thanked.**

1752. **THE COST OF THE BLOCK IS ON THE PAGE AND IS NOT CURED IN IT, AND THE NEW COST IS NAMED ONCE AND NOT ARGUED.** The use of one leg, which takes eleven miles of made road in about four hours before light and about nine otherwise, four hundred paces of lane and about four hundred yards of made road to clean a mile stone, a hot band across the knee by the sixth hour and by the seventh hour on the seventh of Embermonth that did not go down again that day, and four hundred yards of a lane and nine feet of ache in it at the foot of a board. The use of one ear on the side the stick is on, which took a market morning's four hundred people and carts until it sang, and which **this block states as a fact he plans around rather than as a discovery, at `chapter-0789.md`, where he goes up four days of made road with his head turned to the left and the page says that a man who has been half deaf in one ear for about four months has stopped planning around it and started planning with it.** The standing fact that he cannot be near the works without somebody standing with him. **AND THE NEW COST, WHICH IS NAMED ONCE, AT `chapter-0789.md`, AND IS NOT ARGUIED AFTERWARDS: that a thing he said out loud at a table in a market street on the twenty-sixth of Longlight cannot be unsaid, that about four hundred people heard it and about nine hundred people along eleven miles of made road behind that street have still never heard it and will never hear it, that no second saying of it in a second street will put it into them or go back into the first street, and that this is the cost of a market street as a room.** Being the man who went and looked is a job and not an honour and nobody was told so and nobody wrote it down.

1753. **THE TUESDAY CAME AND SHUT NOTHING, AND THE REFUSAL IS PAID AND IS NOT RESOLVED.** The roll printed the twenty-third of Fallowmonth, which is a Tuesday and is day 2878. **What happened on that Tuesday, at `chapter-0787.md`, is that nobody filled in the roll and nobody tore the roll up and no gate in this county was shut on the strength of it and no bar was put on any gate and no hurdle was lifted off any track and no road was shut, and about nine people in that market street told the man of thirty-two so one after another in about nine minutes the next morning, and about four hundred people had walked past those boards the day before without any of it.** About four people in nine counties have been saying since that the refusal was paid on the twenty-third of Fallowmonth and that being paid is not the same as being finished, and that nobody in nine counties has written to anybody about why the fourth line is empty, and a question that has not been asked is a question that has not been refused twice. **THE ROLL IS NOT FILLED IN AND IS NOT DESTROYED AT THE BLOCK'S LAST DATELINE AND A ROAD IS NOT SHUT, and both of those are stated on the page at `chapter-0790.md`.**

1754. **THE HAND-OFF: `outline/volume-16.md` SECTION ELEVEN'S THREE THINGS ARE CARRIED FORWARD IN THIS BLOCK'S OWN TERMS AND NONE OF THE THREE IS CONDUCTED.** **A refusal in writing that is also a refusal to govern** is on the page twice and resolved neither time, being the woman's forty-five words at `chapter-0783.md` and the one sentence on the folded sheet inside the covering paper of the second copy at `chapter-0788.md`, and nobody has said who wrote the sheet and nobody is going to be asked. **A sealed route that somebody has claimed can be made to speak** is carried forward and is not touched, being the roadkeeper of about fifty-two's claim made out loud at `chapter-0780.md`, in thirty words, that a bar will get up that track if a barrow can and that he is not going to be the one who tries it; at `chapter-0784.md` he is at those boards at about the tenth hour with a bar in his hand and says nothing out loud at all that morning and goes down the made road toward the coast at about the eleventh hour without trying anything, and about four people had worked out inside nine seconds that a man who claimed that three weeks ago has nothing to say about a form. **An occupation with a gate on it and a keeper who is a local** is set up in this block's own terms and is not entered, being `chapter-0790.md`, where the badge-man of about fifty-eight stands at the roll about as long as it takes to turn a cart twice over, reads nine lines and a date and writes nothing, and about five people in nine counties have said that about the office at the low end of that street has a counter and a door and a man behind it who was born in that street, and that a keeper who is a local is a gate with a man in it, and that nobody in nine counties has ever asked that man what a gate is for.

1759. **WHAT THE REVIEW OF THIS BLOCK FOUND, IN ONE PARAGRAPH, AND IT IS TWELVE FINDINGS OF WHICH THREE ARE BLOCKING AND NONE OF THEM IS A MATTER OF TASTE.** **ONE,** `state/current.md` asserted that roll-up and not in any block. **TWO,** `state/current.md` and `workspace/volume-16/batch-0005/PROMPT.md` gave opposite orders on the single most consequential next action, being that the state file forbade the Volume 16 close prompt and the prompt required it, and `AGENTS.md` and `PHASE_SYSTEM.md` both back the prompt. **THREE,** `chapter-0783.md` reversed the first two of the three answers to the roll, which `chapter-0782.md` had already fixed in order, and this item at 1749 repeated the reversal against its own three numbered answers immediately above it. **FOUR,** `chapter-0782.md` had two paragraphs cut off mid-sentence. **FIVE,** `chapter-0781.md` printed eleven mile stones where the block prints one. **SIX,** `chapter-0781.md` skipped Longlight and six months in the broom's month chain. **SEVEN,** the badge-man at `chapter-0790.md` walked toward the office at the low end, which is the occupation that 1754 and `state/open-threads.md` 735 both record as set up and not entered. **EIGHT,** a misspelling of Fallowmonth reported in forty-nine places in this block. **NINE,** the `about` band published without its instrument. **TEN,** the closing money coda's list of the figures not in the column presented as complete in all ten chapters. **ELEVEN,** the money collision set not independently reproducible. **TWELVE,** `workspace/volume-16/batch-0005/PROMPT.md` asked for at least eleven datelines in a ten-dateline block.

1760. **WHAT WAS REPAIRED ON THE TEN PAGES, IN SIX CLASSES, AND NOTHING ELSE WAS TOUCHED IN THE PROSE.** **One,** `chapter-0782.md`'s tenth-thing paragraph now carries its whole three-clause census tail, being that five have said that about nine hundred yards of standing broom and about nine feet of it across are the same kind of number in two trades and that neither of them is going to be held 1762. **THE STATE-FILE FIGURES THAT MOVED IN THIS REPAIR PASS, ALL OF THEM, AND THE COUNT THAT DID NOT MOVE AT ALL.** Moved: the ten per-file word counts, the block total from 40,563 to **40,517**, the block mean from 4,056.3 to **4,051.7**, the minimum from 3,707 to **3,689** at `chapter-0786.md`, the maximum from 4,289 to **4,380** at `chapter-0782.md`, the `about` band from 22.85 to 26.71 to **22.80 to 26.84** on the case-sensitive lowercase count, the Volume 16 total from 153,865 to **153,667**, its mean from 3,846 to **3,841**, and the manuscript total from 2,298,516 to **2,298,318**, and **the column now foots: 1,977,530 + 167,121 + 38,524 + 34,211 + 40,415 + 40,517 = 2,298,318.** **DID NOT MOVE: the ten datelines and their weekdays, the three market mornings of 2833, 2852 and 2868, all ten drove counts and all ten drove calendar dates, the stone days, the scar ages, the two silence counts under the ninth thing, the practice counts, the Tuesday offsets, the four printed speeches at thirty-one and forty-two and sixteen and forty-five, all ten money columns, the census at Basis A 304 and Basis B 270 and zero openers on both, the thank family at thirty, the duplicate-paragraph count at thirty-six groups and thirty-seven surplus occurrences over all seven hundred and ninety files and the same thirty-six and thirty-seven over the seven hundred and eighty that stood before this block, the four problems and the four data lines per file.** **EVERY COUNT WAS RE-RUN AFTER THE LAST PROSE EDIT AND NOT BEFORE IT.**

1764. **THE FRAME OF THIS BLOCK, ITS TEN DATELINES, ITS TWO MONTH BOUNDARIES AND ITS SEVENTY-TWO DAYS, ALL DERIVED FROM THE DAY NUMBER AND NOT FROM A ROW.** The block runs days 2899 to 2970 inclusive, which is seventy-two days, and it carries **two** month boundaries and not three, being Wolfmonth the first at day 2916 which is a Friday and is not a market morning, and Frostmonth the first at day 2946 which is a Sunday and is not a market morning, **and both of those are inside the block and no chapter is dated on either of them.** **THE TEN DATELINES AS PRINTED ARE 2899, 2908, 2915, 2924, 2932, 2940, 2948, 2957, 2963 AND 2970, being Embermonth the fourteenth, the twenty-third and the thirtieth, Wolfmonth the ninth, the seventeenth and the twenty-fifth, and Frostmonth the third, the twelfth, the eighteenth and the twenty-fifth, all YR 323. Their weekdays are Tuesday, Thursday, Thursday, Saturday, Sunday, Monday, Tuesday, Thursday, Wednesday and Wednesday, and FOUR OF THE TEN ARE MARKET MORNINGS AND SIX ARE NOT, being 2908, 2915, 2924 and 2957, and the six that are not carry the room over the weigh-house shut behind its own door.** The block opens the way round Batch 0003 had it and not the way round Batch 0004 had it: **day 2899 is a Tuesday with the room shut and the practice running and day 2970 is a Wednesday with the room shut and the practice running**, and 2898 is a Monday and 2969 is a Tuesday, both checked. The market mornings inside the seventy-two days are twenty, being ten Thursdays and ten Saturdays, and fifty-two mornings on which the room is shut behind its own door. **THE PRINTED TABLE AT `outline/volume-16.md` SECTION SEVENTEEN IS WRONG IN FOUR ROWS AND TWO OF THEM ARE BESIDE THIS BLOCK, being 2916 which it prints as a Sunday and which is a Friday, and 2946 which it prints as a Thursday and which is a Sunday, and a writer who took a weekday out of that table would have written ten wrong datelines.**

1765. **THE NINE THINGS AND THE NINE PEOPLE, AND THE ACCOUNTING THAT RESOLVES THE APPARENT CONFLICT BETWEEN TEN THINGS AND NINE HELD.** **There are ten things and about nine people say the number at the fourth hour on the five mornings a week that room is shut, and nine of the ten have a person against them and two voices, and one of the ten has nobody against it and never has, being the tenth, which is nine feet wide and about nine hundred yards long and is on the back of a board in a cart shed four days off.** Eight of the ten were held on the morning before the first of Wolfmonth and two were not, being the ninth, which is the head of that cart track and whose person has said nothing out loud at the head of it since the thirteenth of Hearthmonth, and the tenth. **THE BLOCK'S CLIMAX IS THAT NINE PEOPLE HELD NINE THINGS FOR ELEVEN MORNINGS WITHOUT HIM AND IT IS ON THE PAGE AT `chapter-0791.md`, `chapter-0795.md`, `chapter-0796.md`, `chapter-0799.md` AND `chapter-0800.md`, AND HE IS NOT ONE OF THE TWO ON ANY OF THE ELEVEN.**

1766. **THE ELEVEN MORNINGS, NAMED, AND THEY ARE ALL PRACTICE MORNINGS.** He was on that floor on the first of Wolfmonth, day 2916, a Friday, and he was not on it on any of the eleven mornings after it up to and including the seventeenth of Wolfmonth, day 2932, being **the third, the fourth, the fifth, the sixth, the eighth, the tenth, the eleventh, the twelfth, the thirteenth, the fifteenth and the seventeenth of Wolfmonth**, and each of those eleven is a morning the room is shut and the practice runs. **ON ALL ELEVEN THE NUMBER WAS SAID TWICE BY TWO PEOPLE WHO WERE NOT HIM AND EIGHT OF THE TEN THINGS WERE HELD AND NOTHING ABOUT ANY OF IT WAS WRITTEN DOWN.** On the fifth of Wolfmonth, day 2920, he went with the man of about fifty-four who keeps that weigh-house floor about nine hundred yards off the made road to the ninth of the nine stones at about the fourth hour and the man stood about nine feet off and did not go past the ninth of them either, **and what is beyond the ninth of those nine stones is not on the page and has not been on the page for about a year, and the omission is a decision and not an oversight.**

1767. **THE GATEHOUSE FALLS AND IS NOT SHOWN, AND IT IS NOT IN THIS COUNTY, AND NO CHAPTER ENTERS IT.** **THE NEWS ARRIVES AT `chapter-0795.md`, DAY 2932, A SUNDAY, IN THE ORDINARY POST.** A Crown's cart came up that made road at about the fourth hour with a printed sheet in it, went on four days south without stopping at the boards, and the man in the coat with a Crown's button on it did not get down and did not nail anything up and did not know what was in it. **A carter read the whole of it out loud once in the room at the high end of that market street at about the seventh hour and about nine people there heard all of it and five did not and nobody asked him to read it again.** The sheet says that at about the sixth hour on the fifth of Wolfmonth, twelve days before, people with a Crown's warrant went up **about nine hundred yards of a stair** at a place nine days ride from any town in this county, and that the gate at the top of that stair is off its hinges and the place is held and the stair is held and the people on it are not leaving, and that the warrant is in the Marshal's hand and is signed under it, and that the seat that gave the warrant has not sat for eleven weeks and that **about four hundred places are writing a paper by hand because nobody has a seal to give them**. **About nine people in that room saw nothing of it and nobody else in this county saw anything of it either, and about nine hundred people along eleven miles of made road did not know it had happened until a carter read a sheet out in a room. THE MECHANISM IS NOT EXPLAINED AND NO CHAPTER SAYS WHAT A ROADMARK IS AND NO CHAPTER ENTERS THE GATEHOUSE, AND HE WAS FOUR HOURS OFF ON THE MADE ROAD AT THE FOURTH HOUR OF THAT MORNING STANDING AT THE NINTH OF NINE STONES AND NOT PAST THEM AND HE DID NOT KNOW FOR TWELVE DAYS.**

1768. **THE ANTAGONIST'S NAME IS PRINTED FOR THE SECOND AND LAST TIME IN THIS VOLUME, ONCE, IN A DOCUMENT, AT THE FOOT OF SOMETHING WITH A HAND ON IT, AND NEVER IN A MOUTH.** **It is at `chapter-0797.md`, on a folded sheet inside the covering paper folded over the back of a THIRD copy of the roll, in a clerk's hand this county has had before, and at the foot of that sheet, under the clerk's hand, the Marshal's hand is written out in full as Edric Hunn.** The third copy came up the made road in the ordinary post on the third of Frostmonth, day 2948, was nailed over the second copy without the second being taken down, and was carried up that made road by a man in a coat with a Crown's button on it who did not know what was in it. **THE FOUR LINES ON THAT SHEET ARE A DATE, THE FOURTH LINE OF THE ROLL, A PROPOSAL TO ENTER AS THE KEEPER OF THE ROADS OF THIS COUNTY THE MAN WHO KEEPS THE WEIGH-HOUSE FLOOR BY HIS TRADE AND NOT BY HIS NAME, AND A STATEMENT THAT THE COUNTY'S AGREEMENT IS NOT REQUIRED. THE MAN OF THIRTY-TWO LOOKED AT IT FOR ABOUT AS LONG AS IT TAKES TO TURN A CART TWICE OVER AND DID NOT ASK ABOUT IT AND PUT IT BACK THE WAY IT WAS AND NOBODY IN THAT STREET ASKED HIM ANYTHING AND NOBODY READ IT OUT LOUD IN ANY ROOM IN THAT MARKET TOWN, AND THE FOURTH LINE OF THE ROLL ON ALL THREE COPIES IS EMPTY.** The man of about fifty-four has not been asked about it by anybody in nine counties. **THE PRINTING IS ONE ACROSS ALL FIFTY FILES OF VOLUME 16 AND TWO IN THE VOLUME, WHICH IS THE CAP AT `outline/volume-16.md` SECTION SIXTEEN ITEM FOUR, and `Hunn` on its own is not a printing and the bare surname stands nowhere in the volume.**

1769. **THE NAME BUDGET FOR VOLUME 16 IS SIX AND SIX ARE NOW SPENT, WHICH IS THE LAST OF THE SIX.** **THE ONE NAME THIS BLOCK SPENT IS `Bessie Dellow`, first printed at `chapter-0792.md`, about fifty-one, who keeps the inn at the high end of that market street and keeps a tally-stick.** The name is given the way everything in this county gives a name, being that a carter of about thirty-eight asked her one question, kindly, in his own trade, about what a person does with a second copy of a roll of roads that is nailed up at the low end of the street, and she answered in forty-three words, and then he asked her what to call her because he wanted to know who to ask a second time and she told him in twenty-four words. **THE FULL SIX ARE `Renn Ottery` AND `Pell Arkwright` IN BATCH 0001, `Wyn Fell` AND `Abel Chinn` IN BATCH 0003, `Brida Lenth` IN BATCH 0004 AND `Bessie Dellow` IN BATCH 0005, and there is no name left in Volume 16 and a successor in Volume 17 spends nothing out of this budget.** A capitalised two-word sweep over the fifty files of Volume 16 returns exactly thirteen strings, being those six and `Aren Kest`, `Mara Vey`, `Edric Hunn`, `Orme Skell`, `Pell Arkwright` and `Tarr Lyster` and `Corve Sallow`, of which only `Bessie Dellow` is a first printing in this block. The protagonist is thirty-two and was not recounted.

1770. **AREN KEST IS NOT THE CUSTODIAN OF ANY OF IT, THE VOLUME SAYS SO IN FRONT OF ABOUT FOUR HUNDRED PEOPLE ON A MARKET MORNING, AND NOBODY ARGUES AND NOBODY IS THANKS.** **It is at `chapter-0798.md`, day 2957, the twelfth of Frostmonth, a Thursday and the last market morning of the block, with about four hundred people in that market street and a trestle table set up at the boards and about nine people standing at it.** He said one thing out loud, short, about himself, in a voice about four hundred people could hear, **and it was forty-two words**: *I am not one of the nine and I hold none of the nine, and the fourth line on that board is not mine to fill, and nobody in nine counties has asked me to fill it and nobody is going to.* **ABOUT NINE PEOPLE STOPPED WHERE THEY WERE AND ABOUT FOUR HUNDRED PEOPLE WENT ON AND NOBODY ARGUED WITH HIM ABOUT ANY PART OF IT, AND HE IS NOT THE AUTHOR, THE ANNOUNCER, THE SUMMARISER OR THE FOURTH VOICE OF ANY OF THE THREE ANSWERS TO THE ROLL AND HE DOES NOT RESTATE THEM, AND THE WORD THAT NAMES THE OFFICE IS NOT PRINTED ANYWHERE IN THE VOLUME.**

1771. **THE RELATIONSHIP COMES DUE A FOURTH TIME AND IT IS A THIRD PERSON WORKING INSIDE THE TWO OF THEM AS THOUGH THE ARRANGEMENT WERE ALREADY A FACT OF THE WORK, AND IT IS NOT A DECLARATION.** **At `chapter-0793.md`, day 2915, the thirtieth of Embermonth, a Thursday and a market morning, the copyist of about twenty-six came down those thirteen stairs at about the fifth hour with the lamp that had been standing at the foot of them since the fourth hour and set it on the trestle table by that weigh-house floor where about nine people were standing, and the man of thirty-two was at that table and the woman of about twenty-nine was in the lane with the day-book under her arm, and she put the lamp down in the middle of them, took the wet cloth off the table, shook it out and hung it on the rail, put a dry one on and went back up the stairs, and said nothing to anybody about it, and at about the seventh hour the same lamp went back up those stairs and the room it belongs to was open, and neither of the two of them said one word to each other about the lamp at any point in the day and neither of them looked up when it was set down.** About four people in that lane worked out inside nine seconds what it is and five had not. **THE WORD IS NOT PRINTED, NOTHING GOES IN ANY DOCUMENT, THE FOUR AND A HALF YEARS ARE NEVER DESCRIBED AS FOUR AND A HALF YEARS ABOUT THE TWO OF THEM, NOBODY COMMENTS AND NOBODY IS THANKS, AND THE LEGAL FORM STAYS DEFERRED TO VOLUME 18.** The three earlier beats are the copyist's sheet at `chapter-0782.md`, the word keeper at `chapter-0783.md` and the lamp that was not put out at `chapter-0785.md`. No chapter of this block ends on the two of them.

1772. **THE TWO COSTS THIS BLOCK PUTS ON THE PAGE FOR THE FIRST TIME IN THE VOLUME, BOTH OF THEM PLAIN AND BOTH UNEXPLAINED.** **One, the bleed.** On the fifth of Wolfmonth, day 2920, at about the fourth hour, at the ninth of the nine stones, there was blood on the inside of his left wrist and along the heel of his left hand, **and it is warm and it does not stop when it is pressed and it has not faded by anything anybody has done to it**, and it is twenty days old at `chapter-0796.md`, twenty-eight at `chapter-0797.md`, thirty-seven at `chapter-0798.md`, forty-three at `chapter-0799.md` and fifty days old at `chapter-0800.md`. **HE HAS TOLD NOBODY ABOUT IT, NOBODY IN NINE COUNTIES HAS ASKED HIM, IT IS NOT IN ANY DOCUMENT, IT HAS NEVER BEEN SAID OUT LOUD IN A ROOM, THE MAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-FOUR WAS ABOUT NINE FEET OFF AND DID NOT SEE IT HAPPEN, AND NO PANEL AND NO EXPLANATION AND NO PANEL-SHAPED PARAGRAPH IS PRINTED FOR IT AND NOBODY ELSE ON ANY FLOOR IN THIS COUNTY KNEW.** **Two, the last clear thing he will ever have of his mother.** At the fourth hour of the twenty-fifth of Wolfmonth, day 2940, on a weigh-house floor four days off in the dark with about nine people on it, it came to him that his mother said one sentence to him before he came into this county, **and that he had heard it clearly then and had heard it clearly every morning since including that one, and that from that morning he will not hear it clearly again. HE DID NOT SAY IT OUT LOUD AND HE DID NOT PUT IT IN THE NOTEBOOK AND NOBODY ON THAT FLOOR HAS ANY IDEA IT HAPPENED TO HIM, AND THE EAR ON THE SIDE THE STICK IS ON IS THE SIDE SHE SPOKE FROM AND IS PERMANENT AND IS NOT A DISCOVERY.**

1773. **THE THREE HAND-OFFS OF `outline/volume-16.md` SECTION ELEVEN ARE CARRIED FORWARD OUT OF THIS VOLUME AND NONE OF THEM IS CONDUCTED.** **A refusal in writing that is also a refusal to govern** stands at `chapter-0783.md` in forty-five words in a lane and on the folded sheet inside the covering paper of the second copy at `chapter-0788.md`, and it is still there in the covering paper of the second copy at `chapter-0800.md` in a hand nobody in nine counties has claimed, **and no second letter went out of this county in this block and nobody was asked who wrote the first.** **A sealed route that somebody has claimed can be made to speak** is carried forward at `chapter-0795.md` with the roadkeeper of about fifty-two not in that street, not up that made road since the twenty-fourth of Fallowmonth, not asked about the sheet, and having offered nothing about the bar he said at those boards would get up that track if a barrow could, **and no bar was lifted or tried and no hurdle was touched and nobody went up that cart track a third time.** **An occupation with a gate on it and a keeper who is a local** is set up at `chapter-0798.md` by the badge-man of about fifty-eight coming up that street with a badge and no head over him, standing at the third copy of the roll about as long as it takes to turn a cart twice over, reading about nine lines and a date, writing nothing at all and going back the way he had come, and by the office at the low end of that street having a counter and a door and a man behind it who was born in that street, **and nobody went into that office that morning and nobody asked the man behind that counter what a gate is for.**

Lifting the ceiling is an outline decision and was not taken.

1776. **THE THREE PRINTED SPEECHES OF THIS BLOCK AND THEY ALL FOOT OFF THE WORDS PRINTED BESIDE THEM, AND NO OTHER SPEECH CARRIES A COUNT.** **At `chapter-0792.md`, forty-three words and twenty-four words, being the woman of about fifty-one's answer in the inn and then her name, and at `chapter-0798.md`, forty-two words, being the man of thirty-two at the trestle table.** The three answers to the roll were not given a second time anywhere in this block and no speech in this block summarises them.

1777. **WHAT DID NOT MOVE IN THIS BLOCK, AND IT IS THE WHOLE OF THE PROHIBITION LIST.** No System panel is printed and the volume's one stands alone at `chapter-0776.md` and is seventeen words. **THE VOLUME'S TWO PRINTINGS OF THE ANTAGONIST'S NAME ARE NOW BOTH SPENT AND NEITHER MAY EVER BE PRINTED AGAIN IN THIS PROJECT.** Neither refusal of Volume 15 was reopened, argued with, softened, or put to either of the two men, **and the man of forty-one is not in a scene in any of the ten files, is not asked a second question, says nothing, and does not say the wall answer.** Nobody is past the ninth of the nine stones and the far side of them is not shown and the dead grass in the scar stops at the ninth of them and the omission is not an oversight. **No route was opened, closed or cut, no hurdle was lifted, no hurdle was tried at, no bar was lifted or tried, no stone was laid and the mallet was not picked up, the ninth yard of the wall has not been started, and nobody went up that cart track a third time.** The mechanism is not explained and no chapter says what a roadmark is and the separate leaf of the notebook that says a person is not looked at and no character noticed the warmth of the left palm. No head was filled or ruled, no finding was put under any of the ten questions, the half leaf stays face down, the cloth is still over the bench at the low end of those boards and nobody asked what is under it, **the day-book stands at eleven entries on all ten datelines, the register gains no entry, the fourth ruled head is empty, no fifth head is ruled, and the copyist of about twenty-six ruled no head and has been told about none of it.** The three series are three series and the band figure is not printed. The nine houses at Wethergill did not become a body, the nine households at the mill lane did not become a body, and the nine people who refused the roll on the first of Mudmonth did not become a body, **and nobody was asked any of those nine for an account of having said it, nobody was asked the roadkeeper about the broom straw, and nobody was asked whether she wrote the sentence on the folded sheet.** Nobody is sent to the city four hundred miles off, the shut road four hundred miles off stays shut, the road that went quiet in Thawmonth YR 319 is still quiet, the carter's mate of about twenty-two is not found, and nobody traced who asked the fen county road office for the roll. The four holes in the arrangement are four and none is called small and none is resolved and no fifth was opened. **The roll was not filled in, no name was put against a road, no road was shut on the strength of it and it was not torn up.** The scar is three hundred and ninety days old at the block's last dateline and is not cured and is not going to close over, and the four things about nine feet across carry the noun with the number on every page that prints one. **NOBODY WAS THANKED IN ANY CHAPTER AND IN ANY STATE FILE THIS BLOCK WROTE.**

1778. **THE STATE THE BLOCK LEAVES BEHIND, AND THE FIGURES A SUCCESSOR MAY INHERIT AS FACTS.** **One,** the roll is on the boards with three copies of it and nine empty lines on each and the fourth line of this county's empty, and it is not filled in and it is not torn up. **Two,** the folded sheet inside the covering paper of the second copy is still there in an unclaimed hand and nobody has said who wrote it. **Three,** eleven mornings of the practice were run without him and every one of them was held and nothing about any of it was written down. **Four,** the ear on the side the stick is on is permanent and is the side his mother spoke from and he turns his head to the left for a made road on purpose. **Five,** the bleed is fifty days old at the volume's last page and has not faded and nobody in nine counties knows about it. **Six,** the ninth thing is alive, unnamed, unheld and shut by nine hurdles put there by the people at the end of it who would like them to stay. **Seven,** a stair about nine hundred yards long is held by people with a Crown's warrant and is not in this county and nobody here saw it happen. **The figures a successor must re-derive rather than inherit are the drove, the drove's calendar date, the practice count, the stone count, the scar age and the two silence counts under the ninth thing, and the rule at `state/open-threads.md` 721 is the check that catches the largest class of defect this project has produced.**
